The Clever Young Man

Argued with R﻿—﻿— this morning. He is a type specimen of the clever young man. We both are. Our flowers of speech are often forced hothouse plants, paradoxes and cynicisms fly as thick as driving rain and Shaw is our great exemplar. I could write out an exhaustive analysis of the clever young man, and being one myself can speak from “inspired sources” as the newspapers say.

===Chinese===

轮到我自己上床，我有时提防着自己的官能被睡眠偷走。我几乎怕睡：它叫我忧心——这件将发生在我身上的、妙不可知的事，我得在床上摊开自己等候，还要做一番周备的准备。我不像托马斯·布朗爵士那样，总是乐于向太阳告辞、一觉——必要的话——睡进复活里去。我有时醒着躺，纳闷那位神秘的访客几时来唤我离开这个惊心动魄的世界，他又怎么办这件事；为此我试着守住那个渐进的过程、去理解它：当然是不可能，字面上就自相矛盾。所以我永远不会知道，谁也不会。

7月2日

今晚我大获全胜——你想象不到！笔简直在飞，自动而轻松，一页接一页次序井然。我的文体颤音、斗嘴、滚翻、长啸，变化无穷；你能在其中找到一切最微妙的转调、抑扬与婉转。我的灵感像一道光的横梁从天而降，如舍金纳——直从上帝来，真上帝之真上帝。我在一圈金色的光晕里工作，笔尖划过纸面，迸出电火花。

7月3日

聪明的年轻人

今早同R———争论。他是聪明年轻人的模式标本。我们俩都是。我们的言辞之花常是温室里催出来的，悖论和俏皮话下得像密集的骤雨，萧是我们的大宗师。我能把聪明年轻人写一份穷形尽相的分析，而身为其中一员，我可以像报上说的，从"受启发的内部"发言。

===Original===

A common habit is to underline and memorise short, sharp, witty remarks he sees in books and then on future occasions dish them up for his own self-glorification. If the author be famous he begins, “As —﻿—﻿— says, etc.” If unknown the quotation is quietly purloined. He is always very self-conscious and at the same time very self-possessed and very conceited. You tell me with tonic candour that I am insufferably conceited. In return, I smile, making a sardonic avowal of my good opinion of myself, my theory being that as conceit is, as a rule, implicit and, as a rule, blushingly denied, you will mistake my impudent confession for bluff and conclude there is really something far more substantial and honest beneath my apparent conceit. If, on the other hand, I am conceited, why I have admitted it﻿—I agree with you﻿—but though there is no virtue in the confession being quite detached and unashamed﻿—still you haven’t caught me by the tail. It is very difficult to circumvent a clever young man. He is as agile as a monkey.

His principal concern of course is to arouse and maintain a reputation for profundity and wit. This is done by the simple mechanical formula of antithesis: if you like winkles he proves that cockles are inveterately better; if you admire Ruskin he tears him to ribbons. If you want to learn to swim﻿—as it is safer, he shows it is more dangerous to know how to swim and so on. I know his whole box of tricks. I myself am now playing the clever young man by writing out this analysis just as if I were not one myself.

===Chinese===

他一个通病，是把书里那些短促锋利的妙语画线背熟，日后找机会端出来给自己贴金。作者有名，他就开头："正如———所说，"云云；作者无名，引文就悄悄味下。他永远很自觉，同时又永远很镇定、很自负。你拿有益的坦率告诉我，我自负得叫人受不了。作为回答，我微笑，讥诮地自认对自己看法颇佳；我的理论是：自负照例是默认的、照例红着脸否认的，所以你准会把我的厚脸皮自供当成唬人，断定我这层表面的自负底下，必有一点扎实得多、诚实得多的东西。反过来，我若真自负，我已经认了——我同意你——可这份全然超然、恬不知耻的坦白并没有什么美德——你终究没揪住我的尾巴。要绕倒一个聪明的年轻人非常难。他猴子一样灵。

他当然首要关心的是唤起并维持一个"深刻而机智"的名声。办法就是对仗这条简单的机械公式：你爱吃峨螺，他证明蚶螺百般地好；你敬佩罗斯金，他把他撕成条。你想学游泳——正因为它更安全，他就证明会游泳更危险，如此等等。他这一整套把戏我全知道。我此刻写这份分析，本身就是在一本正经地扮聪明年轻人——装得好像我自己不是似的。

===Original===

You doubt my cleverness? Well, some years ago in R﻿—﻿—’s presence I called —﻿—﻿— “the Rev. Fastidious Brisk”﻿—the nickname be it recalled which Henley gave to Stevenson (without the addition of “Rev.”). At the time I had no intention of appropriating the witticism as I quite imagined R﻿—﻿— was acquainted with it. His unexpected explosion of mirth, however, made me uncomfortably uncertain of this, yet for the life of me I couldn’t muster the honesty to assure him that my feather was a borrowed one. A few weeks later he referred to it again as “certainly one of my better ones”﻿—but still I remained dumb and the time for explanations went for once and all. Now see what a pretty pickle I am in: the name “Brisk” or “F.B.” is in constant use by us for this particular person﻿—he goes by no other name, meanwhile I sit and wonder how long it will be before R﻿—﻿— finds me out. There are all sorts of ways in which he might find out: he might read about it for himself, someone might tell him or﻿—worst of all﻿—one day when we are dining out somewhere he will announce to the whole company my brilliant appellation as a little after-dinner diversion: I shall at once observe that the person opposite me *knows* and is about to air his knowledge; then I shall look sternly at him and try to hold him: he will hesitate and I shall land him with a left and right: “I suppose you’ve read Henley’s verses on Stevenson?” I remark easily and in a moment or so later the conversation has moved on.

August 1

===Chinese===

你怀疑我的聪明？喏，几年前我当着R———的面，管———叫"挑剔·轻快牧师"——请回想，这是亨利给史蒂文森起的绰号（少了"牧师"二字）。当时我并不想冒领这句妙语，因为我满以为R———知道它。可他那声出乎意料的爆笑，笑得我心里发虚；我又实在鼓不起诚实，向他保证我这根羽毛是借来的。几星期后他又提起它，"确是我较好的一批之一"——我还是哑着，解释的机会就此一去不返。现在瞧我陷得多俊：那位仁兄如今在我们嘴里只有"轻快"或"F.B."一个名字——他再没别的称呼——而我坐在那儿盘算，R———还要多久识破我。他识破的路子有的是：他自己可能读到，有人可能告诉他，或者——最糟的——哪天我们在外面吃饭，他当着满座宣布我这个妙号，当作饭后的一个小余兴：我立刻会觉察到对面那位*知道*了、正要卖弄；我便狠狠瞪他，试图镇住他；他会迟疑，我便左右开弓一记重击："我想你读过亨利写史蒂文森的那几首诗吧？"我随口一说，稍顷，话题已然翻页。

8月1日

===Original===

Am getting married at —﻿—﻿— Register Office on September 15th. It is impossible to set down here all the labyrinthine ambages of my will and feelings in regard to this event. Such incredible vacillations, doubts, fears. I have been living at a great rate below surface recently. “If you enjoy only twelve months’ happiness,” the Doctor said to me, “it is worth while.” But he makes a recommendation.﻿ ﻿… At his suggestion E﻿—﻿— went to see him and from his own mouth learnt all the truth about the state of my health, to prevent possible mutual recriminations in the future.18 To marry an introspective dyspeptic﻿—what a prospect for her!﻿ ﻿… I exercise my microscopic analysis on her now as well as on myself.﻿ ﻿… This power in me is growing daily more automatic and more repugnant. It is a nasty morbid unhealthy growth that I want to hide if I cannot destroy. It amounts to being able at will to switch myself in and out of all my most cherished emotions; it is like the case in Sir Michael Foster’s *Physiology* of a man who, by pressing a tumour in his neck could stop or at any rate control the action of his heart.

August 2

House pride in newlywed folk, for example, H. and D. today at Golder’s Green or the Teignmouth folk, is very trying to the bachelor visitor. They will carry a chair across the room as tenderly as though it were a child and until its safe transit is assured, all conversation goes by the board. Or the wife suddenly makes a remark to the husband sotto voce, both thereupon start up simultaneously (leaving the fate of Warsaw undecided) while you, silenced by this unexpected manoeuvre, wilt away in your chair, the pregnant phrase stillborn on your lips. Presently they reenter the room with the kitten that was heard in the scullery or with a big stick used to flourish at a little Tomtit on the rose tree. *She* apologises and both settle down again, recompose their countenances into a listening aspect and with a devastating politeness, pick up the poor, little, frayed-out thread of the conversation where it left off with: “Europe? you were saying.﻿ ﻿…” I mobilise my scattered units of ideas but it is all a little chilly for the lady of the house if she listens with her face and speaks with her lips﻿—her heart is far from me: she fixes a glassy eye on the tip of my cigarette, waiting to see if the ash will fall on her carpet.

August 6

===Chinese===

9月15日，我将在———登记所结婚。这里实在没法写下我的意志和情感围绕这件事的全部迷宫般的迂回。这样难以置信的摇摆、疑惧。近来我在水面以下活得很猛。"哪怕只享受十二个月的幸福，"医生对我说，"也值得。"可他这是做推荐。……在他的建议下，E———去见了他，关于我健康的全部实情，都从他自己口里听了个透，以防日后彼此埋怨。18娶一个内省的消化不良者——于她是怎样的前景！……我如今把显微分析也用在她身上，同用在自己身上一样。……我这门本事一天天更自动、更叫我厌恶。它是个不干净的病态增生，毁不掉就想藏。它厉害到能随意把我自己从我最珍视的情感里拔出、插进；好比迈克尔·福斯特爵士《生理学》里那个病例：有人按压颈上一个瘤，就能叫心跳停下，或至少控制它。

8月2日

新婚夫妇的住宅自豪，比如今天戈尔德斯格林的H.和D.，或廷茅斯那一对，对单身访客实在是一种考验。他们会把一把椅子抬过房间，轻柔得像抬一个孩子，椅子平安落定之前，一切谈话作废。或者太太忽然对丈夫耳语一句，两人同时弹起来（华沙的命运悬在那儿不管），你被这突然的机动噤了声，在椅子里菸掉，到了嘴边的那句警句胎死唇上。过一会儿他们重又进屋，或抱着在洗碗间听见叫唤的小猫，或拿着一根要在玫瑰树上向一只小山雀挥舞的大棍子。*她*道声歉，两人重新落座，重新把脸整理成一副恭听的神情，带着毁灭性的礼貌，捡起那截磨得起毛的可怜线头，从断处接上："欧洲？您方才说。……"我把四散的思路重新集结，可对这家的女主人而言，一切都有点冷：她拿脸听、拿嘴唇说——心早不在我这儿：她把一只玻璃眼钉在我烟头的灰上，专等它掉到她地毯上。

8月6日

===Original===

The most intimate and extensive journal can only give each day a relatively small sifting of the almost infinite number of things that flow through the consciousness. However vigilant and artful a diarist may be, plenty of things escape him and in any event recollection is not recreation.﻿ ﻿…

To keep a journal is to have a secret liaison of a very sentimental kind. A *journal intime* is a super-confidante to whom everything is told and confessed. For an engaged or married man to have a secret super-confidante who knows things which are concealed from his lady seems to me to be deliberate infidelity. I am as it were engaged to two women and one of them is being deceived. The word “Deceit” comes up against me in this double life I lead, and insists I shall name a plain thing bluntly. There is something very like sheer moral obliquity in these entries behind her back.﻿ ﻿… Is this journal habit slowly corrupting my character? Can an engaged or married man conscientiously continue to write his *journal intime*?

This question of giving up my faithful friend after September I must consider.

Of course most men have something to conceal from someone. Most married men are furtive creatures, and married women too. But I have a Gregers Werle-like passion for life to be lived on a foundation of truth in every intercourse. I would have my wife know all about me and if I cannot be loved for what I surely am, I do not want to be loved for what I am not. If I continue to write therefore she shall read what I have written.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

再亲密、再详尽的日记，也只能从每天流过意识的近乎无限的事物里，筛出相对少少的一点。日记家再警觉、再有手段，漏网的总多，何况回忆并非重造。……

记日记，就是维持一桩缠绵的私情。一本*私日记*是一位超级知己，一切都向它倾诉、招认。一个订了婚或结了婚的人，养着一位瞒着自家女士的超级知己——她知道那些被瞒着的事——在我看来，这无异于蓄意的背信。我仿佛同两个女人订了婚，其中一个正受着骗。"欺骗"这个词迎面撞上我，逼我把一件东西直呼其名。背着她的这些条目里，实在有几分不折不扣的道德歪斜。……这日记的习惯，是不是在慢慢腐化我的品格？一个订了婚或结了婚的人，还能凭良心把他的*私日记*记下去吗？

九月之后要不要同这位忠心的朋友分手，这个问题我必须考虑。

当然，多数人都有事瞒着某人。多数已婚男子是鬼祟的动物，已婚女子也一样。可我有格瑞格·维勒那样的激情，要人生的一切往来都建立在真实的地基上。我要我的妻子知晓我的全部；倘若我不能因我确实所是而被爱，我也不愿因我所不是而被爱。所以我若继续写，她就将读我写下的全部。……

===Original===

My Journal keeps open house to every kind of happening in my soul. Provided it is a veritable autochthon﻿—I don’t care how much of a tatterdemalion or how ugly or repulsive﻿—I take him in and﻿—I fear sponge him down with excuses to make him more creditable in other’s eyes. You may say why trouble whether you do or whether you don’t tell us all the beastly little subterranean atrocities that go on in your mind. Any eminently “right-minded” *Times* or *Spectator* reader will ask: “Who in Faith’s name is interested in your introspective muck-rakings﻿—in fact, who the Devil are you?” To myself, a person of vast importance and vast interest, I reply﻿—as are other men if I could but understand them as well. And in the firm belief that whatever is inexorably true however unpleasant and discreditable (in fact true things can never lack a certain dignity), I would have you know Mr. *Times*- and Mr. *Spectator*-reader that actual crimes have many a time been enacted in the secrecy of my own heart and the only difference between me and an habitual criminal is that the habitual criminal has the courage and the nerve and I have not. What, then, may these crimes be? Nothing much﻿—only murders, theft, rape, etc. None of them, thank God! fructify in action﻿—or at all events only the lesser ones. My outward and visible life if I examine it is merely a series of commonplace, colourless and thoroughly average events. But if I analyse myself, my inner life, I find I am both incredibly worse and incredibly better than I appear. I am Christ and the Devil at the same time﻿—or as my sister once called me﻿—a child, a wise man, and the Devil all in one. Just as no one knows my crimes so no one knows of my good actions. A generous impulse seizes me round the heart and I am suddenly moved to give a poor devil a £5 note. But no one knows this because by the time I come to the point I find myself handing him a sixpenny-bit and am quite powerless to intervene. Similarly my murders end merely in a little phlegm.

August 7

Two Adventures

On a bus the other day a woman with a baby sat opposite, the baby bawled, and the woman at once began to unlace herself, exposing a large, red udder, which she swung into the baby’s face. The infant, however, continued to cry and the woman said﻿—

“Come on, there’s a good boy﻿—if you don’t, I shall give it to the gentleman opposite.”

===Chinese===

我的日记对灵魂里发生的一切门户大开。只要它是土生土长的真货——我不在乎它多褴褛、多丑、多可憎——我一律收留，而且——恐怕还要拿辩解给它擦洗，好教它在旁人眼里体面些。你要问：你把脑子里那些龌龊的地下暴行说与不说给我们听，有什么要紧。任何一位品行端正的《泰晤士报》或《旁观者》读者都会问："到底谁稀罕你这些内省的扒粪——说到底，你算哪根葱？"而对本人——一个无比重要、无比有趣的人——我答：旁人也个个如此，只要我能像懂自己一样懂他们。并且我坚信，凡是无情真实的东西，无论多么不快、多么丢脸（真实的东西其实从不缺几分尊严），所以，*泰晤士报*先生、*旁观者*先生，我要你们知道：真正的罪行，在我自己心里的密室里，已上演过许多回；我与惯犯的唯一区别，是惯犯有胆量有神经，而我没有。那么，这些罪行是什么？没什么大不了——不过凶杀、盗窃、强奸等等。感谢上帝，一概没有落实为行动——或者至多只落实了轻的几桩。我的外在与可见的一生，若细看，只是一串平常、无色、彻头彻尾中等的事件。可我一分析自己、分析我的内心生活，就发现自己比外表坏到不可信，又好到不可信。我同时是基督与魔鬼——或者如我妹妹有回说的——孩子、智者和魔鬼三位一体。正如没人知道我的罪行，也没人知道我的善举。一股慷慨接住我的心，我忽然动了念头，要给一个可怜虫一张五镑票。可没人知道这个，因为到了跟前，我发现自己递过去的是一枚六便士，而且全然无力干预。同样，我的凶杀到头来也只落得一口痰。

8月7日

两桩奇遇

前几天在公共汽车上，对面坐着一个抱婴儿的女人，婴儿大哭，女人立刻解衣襟，亮出一只肥大的红乳房，朝婴儿脸上一荡。婴儿照哭不误，女人便说——

"来，乖孩子——再不吃，我就给对面这位先生吃了。"

===Original===

Do I look ill-nourished?

---

“*Arma virumque cano*,” a beggar said to me this morning in the High Street, “or as the boy said, ‘Arms and the man with a dog,’ mistaking the verb for the noun. Oh! yes, sir, I remember my Latin. Of course, I feel it’s rather invidious my coming to you like this, but everything is absolutely *non est* with me,” and so on.

“My dear sir,” I answered expansively, “I am as poor as you are. You at least have seen better days you say﻿—but I never have.”

He changed in a minute his cringing manner and rejoined:

“No, I shouldn’t think you had,” eyeing me critically and slinking off.

Am I *so* shabby?

August 8

By Jove! I hope I live!﻿ ﻿… Why does an old crock like myself go on living? It causes me genuine amazement. I feel almost ashamed of myself because I am not yet dead seeing that so many of my full-blooded contemporaries have perished in this War. I am so grateful for being allowed to live so long that nothing that happens to me except death could upset me much. I should be happy in a coal mine.

August 12

Suffering from indigestion. The symptoms include:

-

Excessive pandiculation,

-

Excessive oscitation,

-

Excessive eructation,

-

Dyspnoea,

-

Sphygmic flutters,

-

Abnormal *porrigo*,

-

A desiccated epidermis.

August 16

Lice or “Creeping Ferlies”19

===Chinese===

我看上去像营养不良吗？

---

"*Arma virumque cano*（我歌颂战争与人），"今早在海街上一个乞丐对我说，"或者像那孩子说的，'狗和拿枪的男人'，把动词当成了名词。哦！是的，先生，我的拉丁文我还记得。当然，我这副样子来找您，实在有些扎眼，可我的一切都*不复存在*了，"如此等等。

"亲爱的先生，"我襟怀大开地答道，"我同您一样穷。您好歹见过好日子——我可是从来没见过。"

他那副谄媚的嘴脸霎时变了，回敬道：

"是啊，我看您也没见过，"把我从头到脚一打量，缩着脖儿走了。

我就这么*寒酸*吗？

8月8日

老天！但愿我活下去！……我这样一副老骨头架子怎么还活着？这叫我由衷地惊异。那么多血气方刚的同辈都死在这场战争里了，我竟还没死，我几乎替自己害臊。能被容许活这么久，我感激不尽，以致除了死，再没什么能大乱我的心。叫我下煤窑我也快活。

8月12日

消化不良。症状包括：

-

伸懒腰过多，

-

打哈欠过多，

-

嗳气过多，

-

呼吸困难，

-

脉来颤动，

-

*头皮屑*异常，

-

表皮干燥失水。

8月16日

虱子，或曰"爬行的怪物"19

===Original===

I probably know more about Lice than was ever before stored together within the compass of a single human mind! I know the Greek for Louse, the Latin, the French, the German, the Italian. I can reel off all the best remedies for Pediculosis: I am acquainted with the measures adopted for dealing with the nuisance in the field by the German Imperial Board of Health, by the British R.A.M.C., by the armies of the Russians, the French, the Austrians, the Italians. I know its life history and structure, how many eggs it lays and how often, the anatomy of its brain and stomach and the physiology of all its little parts. I have even pursued the Louse into ancient literature and have read old medical treatises about it, as, for example, the *De Phthiriasi* of Gilbert de Frankenau. Mucius the lawgiver died of this disease so also did the Dictator Scylla, Antiochus Epiphanes, the Emperor Maximilian, the philosopher Pherecydes, Philip II of Spain, the fugitive Ennius, Callisthenes, Alcman and many other distinguished people including the Emperor Arnauld in 899. In 955, the Bishop of Noyon had to be sewn up in a leather sack before he could be buried.20 In Mexico and Peru, a poll-tax of Lice was exacted and bags of these treasures were found in the Palace of Montezuma.21 In the *United Service Magazine* for 184222 is an account of the wreck of the *Wager*, a vessel found adrift, the crew in dire straits and Captain Cheap lying on the deck﻿—“like an anthill.”

So that as an ancient writer puts it, “you must own that for the quelling of human pride and to pull down the high conceits of mortal man, this most loathsome of all maladies (Pediculosis) has been the inheritance of the rich, the wise, the noble and the mighty﻿—poets, philosophers, prelates, princes, Kings and Emperors.”

In his well-known *Bridgewater Treatise*, the Rev. Dr. Kirby, the Father of English Entomology, asked: “Can we believe that man in his pristine state of glory and beauty and dignity could be the receptacle of prey so loathsome as these unclean and disgusting creatures?”23 He therefore dated their creation *after* the Fall.

===Chinese===

关于虱子，我懂得的，恐怕比先前任何一颗人类头脑所装下的都多！虱子的希腊文、拉丁文、法文、德文、意大利文我都知道。虱病的各种最佳疗法我能一口气背出：德国帝国卫生局、英国皇家陆军医疗队、俄军、法军、奥军、意军在战地对付这害虫的各项措施，我无一不晓。我通晓它的生活史与构造，产几枚卵、多久产一回，它的脑和胃的解剖，它各小器官的生理。我甚至把虱子追进了古代文献，读过关于它的古老医论，比如吉尔伯特·德·弗兰克瑙的《虱病论》。立法者穆齐乌斯死于斯病，独裁者苏拉、安条克·伊皮法尼斯、皇帝马克西米利安、哲学家费雷居德斯、西班牙的腓力二世、亡命者恩尼乌斯、卡利斯提尼、阿尔克曼等等显贵亦然，还包括899年的皇帝阿尔诺。955年，努瓦永主教下葬前不得不先缝进一只皮口袋。20在墨西哥和秘鲁，虱子要纳人头税，蒙特祖玛宫殿里就发现过一袋袋这种珍宝。21 1842年的《联合勤务杂志》22 上有一段"韦杰号"失事的记载：那船被发现漂在海上，船员濒绝，奇普船长躺在甲板上——"像一座蚁丘"。

所以，正如一位古代作家所言："你们必须承认，为镇压人类的骄傲、挫抑凡人的高自期许，这一切病症中最可憎者（虱病），向来是富人、智者、贵人与强人——诗人、哲人、主教、王侯、国王与皇帝——的遗产。"

英国昆虫学之父柯比牧师博士，在他那部著名的《布里奇沃特论》里问："我们能够相信，处于原初荣耀、美善与尊严中的人，竟是如此不洁可憎的寄生毒物的容受者吗？"23于是他把它们的受造定在*堕落之后*。

===Original===

The other day a member of the staff of the Lister Institute called to see me on a lousy matter, and presently drew some live Lice from his waistcoat pocket for me to see. They were contained in pill boxes with little bits of muslin stretched across the open end through which the Lice could thrust their little hypodermic needles when placed near the skin. He feeds them by putting these boxes into a specially constructed belt and at night ties the belt around his waist and all night sleeps in Elysium. He is not married.

In this fashion, he has bred hundreds from the egg upwards and even hybridised the two different species!

In the enfranchised mind of the scientific naturalist, the usual feelings of repugnance simply do not exist. Curiosity conquers prejudice.

August 27

Am spending my summer holidays in the Lakes at Coniston with G﻿—﻿— and R﻿—﻿—.﻿ ﻿… I am simply consumed with pride at being among the mountains at last! It is an enormous personal success to have arrived at Coniston!

August 29

Climbed a windy eminence on the other side of the Lake and had a splendid view of Helvellyn﻿—like a great hog’s back. It is fine to walk over the elastic turf with the wind bellowing into each ear and swirling all around me in a mighty sea of air until I was as clean-blown and resonant as a seashell. I moved along as easily as a disembodied spirit and felt free, almost transparent. The old earth seemed to have soaked me up into itself, I became dissolved into it, my separate body was melted away from me, and Nature received me into her deepest communion﻿—until, *until* I got on the lee side of a hedge where the calm brought me back my gaol of clay.

September 1

Fourteen days hence I shall be a married man. But I feel most dejected about it. When I fell down the other day, I believe I slightly concussed my spinal column, with the result that my 1913 trouble has returned, but this time on the *left* side! paralysis and horrible vertigo and presentiments of sudden collapse as I walk.

September 2

I fear I have been overdoing it in this tempting mountain region. Walking too far, etc. So I am slacking. It was fortunate I did not get concussion of the brain﻿—I came within an inch of it: the hair of my head brushed the ground.

A Buxom Rogue in Earthenware

===Chinese===

前几天，利斯特研究所的一位职员为一件生虱的事来见我，谈着谈着，从背心口袋里摸出几只活虱给我看。它们养在丸药盒里，敞口绷一小块细纱，凑近皮肤时，虱子好把小小的皮下针管伸出去。他喂它们的法子，是把这些盒子装进一条特制的腰带，夜里系在腰上，整夜睡在极乐世界里。他没结婚。

就这样，他从卵起育出了成百只，甚至让两个不同的种杂交了！

在科学博物学者获得解放的心智里，寻常那种憎厌根本不存在。好奇征服偏见。

8月27日

同G———和R———在湖区科尼斯顿度暑假。……我终于到了群山之中，简直被骄傲烧透了！抵达科尼斯顿，是一件莫大的个人成就！

8月29日

爬上湖对岸一座迎风的高地，把赫尔维林看了个痛快——像一条大猪的脊背。踏着有弹性的草皮走，风朝两只耳朵里吼，在我周身一片空气的大海里旋，直到我像一枚海螺被吹得透亮、嗡嗡作响，何等痛快。我像一缕脱离肉身的精魂那样轻移，自觉自由，几乎透明。老大地仿佛把我吸进了它自己，我在它里面消融，我这个孤立的身子从我身上化开，自然把我接进她最深的领圣餐里——直到，*直到*我躲到一道树篱的背风面，那份平静把我的泥牢还给了我。

9月1日

再过十四天我就是已婚男人了。可我心里万分沮丧。前几天我摔了一跤，想必轻微震伤了脊柱，1913年的老毛病回来了，这回却在*左*边！走路时半身麻痹、可怕的眩晕、随时会猝倒的预感。

9月2日

怕是在这片诱人的山乡做得过了头。走得太远等等。所以我在歇着。万幸没摔成脑震荡——就差一寸：我头上的头发都擦着地了。

陶塑的丰乳小泼妇

===Original===

I knocked at the door of Sunbeam Cottage the other morning to know if they had a boat for hire. The door was promptly opened by a plump, charming little wench of about 17, and I caught a glimpse of the kitchen with its gunrack holding two fowling pieces, a grandfather clock in one corner and a dresser full of blueish china.

“We don’t let our boat out for hire,” she answered with a smile so honest and natural and spontaneous that I was already saying to myself I had never met with anything like it at all when she stretched up her bare, dairymaid arm﻿—strong, creamy and soft, just reached a big key strung to a wooden block and lying on the top shelf of the dresser and at once handed it to me with:

“But you are quite welcome to use it and here is the key to the boathouse.”

I now felt certain that she was one in a million and thanked her most awfully. I have never met such swiftly-moving generosity.

“It’s very nice on the Lake just now,” she said. “I like to lie in the boat with a book and let her drift.”

I asked her if she would not come too, but this tight little fairy was too busy in the house. She is Clara Middleton done in earthenware.

Subsequently R﻿—﻿— and I often visited the cottage and we became great friends, her mother showing us some letters she received as a girl from John Ruskin﻿—a great friend of hers. The gamekeeper himself said that for his part he could never read Ruskin’s books﻿—it was like driving a springless cart over a rocky road. We all laughed and I said he was prejudiced in view of the letters which began: “My darling,” and finished up “Yr loving J. R.”

But Mrs. —﻿—﻿— said he had never read them, and Madge (ah! that name!) said her father had never shown the least interest in them at which we laughed again, and the gamekeeper laughed too. He is such a jolly man﻿—they all are delightfully simple, charming folk and we talked of Beasts and Birds that live on the mountains.

September 4

Bathed in the Lake from the boat. It was brilliantly fine. R﻿—﻿— dipped her paddles in occasionally just to keep the boat from grounding. Then I clambered over the bows and stood up to dry myself in the sun like one of Mr. Tuke’s young men.

September 7

===Chinese===

那天早上我去敲"阳光小舍"的门，问他们有没有船出租。门应声开了，开门的是个约摸十七岁的丰满俏丽的小妞，我瞥见厨房里枪架上架着两支猎枪，墙角一座落地爷爷钟，碗柜里满摆着发蓝的瓷器。

"我们的船不外租，"她答，那微笑诚实、自然、浑然天成，我正自忖从没见过这样的笑，她已伸出那只挤奶姑娘的光胳膊——结实、奶油似的软——刚好够到穿在木牌上的一把大钥匙，从碗柜顶格上取下，递到我手里：

"不过您尽管用，这是船屋的钥匙。"

我这时已断定她是千里挑一，千恩万谢。我从没见过这样爽快的慷慨。

"湖上这会儿正好，"她说，"我爱躺在船里看书，由它漂。"

我问她肯不肯同去，可这位紧致的小仙女家里太忙。她是陶土烧成的克拉拉·米德尔顿。

后来R———和我常去那小舍，成了很好的朋友。她母亲给我们看了她做姑娘时收到的约翰·罗斯金的信——他是她的挚友。猎场看守人自己说，他那个人是读不了罗斯金的书的——像赶一辆没弹簧的车走石头路。我们都笑，我说他存心偏心，因为那些信开头是"亲爱的"，落款是"爱你的J. R."。

可———太太说他从来没读过，玛琪（啊！这名字！）说她父亲对那些信从没显过一点兴趣，我们又笑了，猎场看守人也笑。他是个顶快活的人——他们全家都是可爱纯朴的人家，我们同他们聊山里的走兽和飞禽。

9月4日

从船上跳进湖里洗澡。天气晴好极了。R———不时划几桨，免得船搁浅。随后我翻过船头爬上去，站在太阳里晾干自己，活像塔克先生笔下的年轻人。

9月7日

===Original===

My 26th birthday. In London again. Went straight to the Doctor and reported myself. I quite expected him to forbid the marriage as I could scarcely hobble to his house. To my amazement, he apparently made light of my paralysis, said it was a common accident to bruise the os coccyx, etc.

September 8

Am staying at —﻿—﻿— for a few days to rest and try to be better by that fateful 11th, when I am married.

*Later*: My first experience of a zeppelin raid. Bombs dropped only a quarter of a mile away and shrapnel from the guns fell on our roof. We got very panicky and went into a neighbour’s house, where we cowered down in our dressing-gowns in absolute darkness while bombs exploded and the dogs barked.

I was scared out of my life and had a fit of uncontrollable trembling. Later we rang up —﻿—﻿— and —﻿—﻿—, and thank Heavens both are safe. A great fire is burning in London, judging by the red glare. At midnight sat and drank sherry and smoked a cigar with Mr. —﻿—﻿—, my braces depending from my trousers like a tail and showing in spite of dressing-gown. Then went home and had some neat brandy to steady my heart. H﻿—﻿— arrived soon after midnight. A motor-omnibus in Whitechapel was blown to bits. Great scenes in the city.

September 9

Very nervy today. Hobbled down the road to see the damage done by the bombs.

September 10

A swingeing cold in the head through running about on the night of the raid. Too feeble to walk far, so Mrs. —﻿—﻿— went into the town for me and purchased my wedding-ring, which cost £2 5s. 0d.

===Chinese===

我的二十六岁生日。又回了伦敦。径直去医生处报到。我满以为他会禁止这桩婚事，因为我简直是一瘸一拐挪到他家的。岂料他竟对我的麻痹轻描淡写，说跌伤尾骨是常有的事，等等。

9月8日

在———小住几天，想在那要命的11号之前好起来——那天我结婚。

*后来*：头一回经历齐柏林空袭。炸弹就落在四分之一英里外，高射炮的弹片落在我们屋顶上。我们慌了神，躲进邻居家，裹着晨衣在漆黑里缩成一团，外头炸弹炸响，狗吠成一片。

我吓得魂飞魄散，一阵控制不住的发抖。后来给———和———打了电话，谢天谢地，两位都平安。看那片红光，伦敦正烧着一场大火。半夜里我同———先生坐着喝了雪利、抽了雪茄，我的背带从裤腰垂下来像条尾巴，晨衣也遮不住。随后回家灌了点纯白兰地稳住心。H———午夜刚过就到了。白教堂一辆公共汽车被炸成了碎片。城里场面盛大。

9月9日

今天神经很坏。一瘸一拐挪到街口，看炸弹炸出的毁伤。

9月10日

空袭那晚跑来跑去，害了重感冒。弱得走不动远路，只好由———太太进城替我买了结婚戒指，花了两镑五先令。

## Part / 部
===Original===

III

Marriage

September 12

This evening we walked through the Churchyard reading tombstone inscriptions. What a lot of men have had wives!

I can’t make out what has come over folk recently: the wit, wisdom and irony on the old tombstones have given place to maudlin sentiment and pious Bible references. Then on the anniversary of the death the custom among poorer classes is to publish such pathetic doggerel as the following﻿—cuttings I have taken from time to time from the local newspaper in —﻿—﻿—:

“Her wish:

“ ‘Farewell dear brother, Mother, sisters,

My life was passed in love for thee.

Mourn not for me nor sorrow take

But love my husband for my sake

Until the call comes home to thee,

Live thou in peace and harmony.’ ”

Again:

“A day of remembrance sad to recall

But still in my heart he is loved best of all

No matter how I think of him﻿—his name I oft recall;

There is nothing left to answer me but his photo on the wall.”

Or:

“One year has passed since that sad day,

When one we loved was called away.

God took her home; it was His will,

Forget her?﻿—No, we never will.”

These piteous screeds fill me with loving-kindness and with contempt alternately in a pendulum-like rhythm. What is the truth about them? Is the grief of these people as mean and ridiculous as their rhymes? Or is it a pitiful inarticulateness? Or is it merely vulgar advertisement of their sorrow? Or does it signify a passionate intention never to forget?﻿—or a fear of forgetting, the rhymes being used as a fillip to the memory? Or﻿—most miserable of all﻿—is it just a custom, and one followed in order to appear respectable in others’ eyes? Are they poor souls? or contemptible fools?

September 14

There is a ridiculous Cocker spaniel at the house where we are staying. He must have had a love affair and been jilted, or else he’s a sort of village idiot. The landlady says he’s not so silly as he looks﻿—but he looks very silly: he languishes sentimentally, and when we laugh at him he looks “hurt.” Today we took him up on the Down and it seemed to brighten him up. Really, he is sane enough, with plenty of common sense and good manners. But he is kept at home in the garden so much, lolling about all day, that as E﻿—﻿— said, having nothing to do, he falls in love.

---

The *Saturday Review* writes:

===Chinese===

III

婚姻

9月12日

今晚我们从教堂墓园里穿行而过，一路读着墓碑上的铭文。有过妻子的男人可真多啊！

我真弄不明白近来人们是怎么了：老墓碑上的机智、睿智与讥诮，已让位于滥情的伤感与虔诚的圣经引文。而到了忌日，穷苦人家有个习俗，是在报上刊出诸如下面这般可怜的打油诗——都是我不时从————的当地报纸上剪存的：

“她的心愿：

“‘别了，亲爱的哥哥、母亲、妹妹们，
我的一生在对你的爱中度过。
莫为我哀恸，莫为我伤悲，
看在我的份上，请爱我的丈夫，
直到那召唤临到你身上，
愿你平安度日，和睦为怀。’”

再来一首：

“追忆之日，思之恸然，
但在心中，最爱的仍是他。
无论怎样想念他——他的名字我时时念起；
应答我的，只剩墙上那张照片。”

或者：

“自那悲伤之日，一年已过，
我们挚爱的人蒙召离世。
上帝接她归家；此乃祂的旨意，
忘了她？——不，我们永不忘记。”

这些哀哀切切的歪诗，让我像钟摆一样在怜爱与轻蔑之间来回摆动。这些诗背后究竟是何真相？这些人的悲恸，当真如他们的韵文一样卑琐可笑么？抑或是一种可怜的辞不达意？抑或不过是对自身悲伤的庸俗张扬？抑或是一种永志不忘的热切心意？——还是对遗忘的恐惧，韵文被用作唤起记忆的提神之刺？抑或——最可悲的一种——仅仅是一种习俗，遵行它只是为了在旁人眼中显得体面？他们是可怜的灵魂？还是可鄙的蠢货？

9月14日

我们借住的那户人家有一只可笑的可卡犬。它准是谈过一场恋爱又被甩了，要不然就是村里的呆子那一类。女房东说它不像看上去那么傻——可它看上去确实傻得很：它多愁善感地憔悴着，我们笑它时，它还露出一副“受了伤”的神气。今天我们带它上丘原去，它似乎精神了不少。其实它够清醒的，有的是常识和良好教养。只是它成天被关在家里的花园里，无所事事地闲荡，正如E——所说，没事可做，便谈起恋爱来了。

---

《周六评论》（*Saturday Review*）写道：

===Original===

The effect of the “Brides and the Bath” Case on people with any trace of *nice feeling* is perhaps not particularly mischievous, though the thing is repulsive and hateful to them.﻿ ﻿… To gloat over the details of repulsive horrors, simply *from motives of curiosity*﻿—this is bad and degrading.

What a lot of repulsive things the nice refined people who read the *Saturday Review* must find in the world just now. For example the War. “Simply from motives of curiosity.” Why certainly, no other than these, concerning one of the most remarkable murders in the annals of crime. And murders anyhow are damned interesting﻿—which the *Saturday Review* isn’t.

Chipples

I was surprised to discover the other day that when I talked of Chipples no one understood what I meant! It proves to be a dialect word familiar to all residents in Devonshire and designating spring onions. Anyway you won’t find it in Murray’s Dictionary; yet etymologically it is an extremely interesting word and a thoroughly good word with a splendid pedigree. To wit:

Italian: *Cipollo*.

Spanish: *Cebolla*.

French: *Ciboule*.

Latin: *Caepulla*, dim. of *caepa* (cf. *cive*, *civot*).

Now how did this pretty little alien manage to settle down among simple Devon folk? What has been the relation between Italy and﻿—say Appledore, or Plymouth?24

October 6

In London once more, living at her flat and using her furniture.

The Chalcidoidea

The Chalcidoidea are minute winged insects that parasitise other insects, and in the *Memoirs of the Queensland Museum* (Vol. I, 1912) you shall find an enormous catalogue of them by a person named Girault who writes the following dedication:

“I respectfully dedicate this little portion of work to science, common sense or true knowledge. I am convinced that human welfare is so dependent upon science that civilisation would not endure without it, and that what is meant by progress would be impossible. Also I am convinced that the great majority of mankind are too ignorant, that education is too archaic and impractical as looked at from the standpoint of intrinsic knowledge. There is too little known of the essential unity of the Universe and of things included, for instance, man himself. Opinions and prejudices rule in the place of what is true.﻿ ﻿…”

Part II is dedicated to:

===Chinese===

“浴缸新娘”案对那些尚存一丝*高雅情操*的人，影响或许算不上特别坏，尽管这桩事在他们看来可憎可厌。……仅仅*出于好奇的动机*，便去咂摸那些令人作呕的恐怖细节——这是坏事，是堕落。

这些读《周六评论》的文雅高贵的先生们，眼下在这世上想必发现了多少可憎的东西啊。譬如这场战争。“仅仅出于好奇的动机。”可不是嘛，对于犯罪编年史上最引人瞩目的谋杀案之一，除了好奇还能有什么别的动机。何况谋杀案本来就他妈的有趣得很——可《周六评论》就没这份趣味。

奇普尔斯（Chipples）

前些天我惊讶地发现，我一说起“奇普尔斯”，竟没人懂我的意思！原来这是个方言词，德文郡（Devonshire）的住户个个熟稔，指的就是小葱。反正你在默里的词典里是查不到的；然而从词源上看，这是个极有意思的词，是个不折不扣的好词，出身名门。且看：

意大利语：*Cipollo*。
西班牙语：*Cebolla*。
法语：*Ciboule*。
拉丁语：*Caepulla*，*caepa*的指小词（参较*cive*、*civot*）。

那么，这个漂亮的小小外来客，是怎么在淳朴的德文人中间落户的呢？意大利与——譬如说阿普尔多尔，或普利茅斯——之间曾有过怎样的往来？24

10月6日

又回到伦敦，住在她的公寓里，用的是她的家具。

小蜂总科

小蜂总科（Chalcidoidea）是寄生在其他昆虫身上的微小有翅昆虫，在《昆士兰博物馆纪要》（*Memoirs of the Queensland Museum*，第一卷，1912年）里，你可以找到一个名叫吉罗（Girault）的人编写的一份洋洋大观的目录，此人写下了如下题献：

“我谨将这一小部分工作敬献给科学、常识亦即真知。我深信，人类福祉如此仰赖科学，没有科学，文明便无从延续，所谓进步也将无从谈起。我还深信，人类的绝大多数太过无知，从内在知识的立场看，教育太过陈旧而不切实际。对于宇宙的本质统一性，对于宇宙所包罗的万物——譬如人自身——人们所知太少。意见与偏见取代了真实的东西，在那里当道。……”

第二部分则题献给：

===Original===

“The genius of mankind, especially to that form of it expressed in monistic philosophy, whose conceived perception is the highest attainment reached by man.”

I can only echo Whistler’s remark one day as he stood before an execrably bad drawing: “God bless my soul”﻿—uttered slowly and thoughtfully and then repeated.

The beauty of it is that the Editor adds a serious footnote, dissociating himself, and a Scarabee to whom I showed the Work, read it with a clouded brow and then said: “I think it rather out of place in a paper of this sort.”

(Tableau.)

October 12

Down with influenza.

October 13

A Zeppelin raid last night. I am down with a temperature, but our little household remained quite calm, thank God. We heard guns going off, and I had a fit of trembling as I lay in bed. Many dead of heart failure owing to the excitement.

October 14

Still in bed. No raid last night. There were two raids on Wednesday, one at 9:30, and another at midnight. The first time the caretaker of the flats came up very alarmed to say “Zeppelins about,” so we put out the lights. Then at midnight when everyone else was asleep I heard a big voice shout up from the street: “Lights out there. They’re about again.” Lay still in bed and waited. Distant gunfire.

October 17

Bad heart attack.

October 18

Heart intermits. Every three or four minutes. M﻿—﻿— said that I ought to be getting used to it by now! Phew!! Very nervy and pusillanimous. Taking strychnine in strong doses. I hope dear E﻿—﻿— does not catch the flu. She swallows quinine with large hopes.

October 19

Staying at R﻿—﻿—. Had a ghastly journey down, changing trains twice at Clapham Junction and at Croydon, heart intermitting all the time in every position. Poor E﻿—﻿— with me. Today surprised to find myself still alive.

October 20

Better today. After much persuasion, I have got E﻿—﻿— to let the flat so that we can get away into the country outside the zeppelin zone.

October 24

Back in London again. Am better, bolstered up with arsenic and strychnine. Too nervously excited to do any work.

October 25

The letting of our flat is now in the hands of an agent, and E﻿—﻿—, poor dear, is quite resigned to abandoning all her precious wallpapers, etc.

November 7

The flat is let and we are now living in rooms at —﻿—﻿—, 20 miles out of London, to the Westward.

November 8

===Chinese===

“献给人类的天才，尤其是以一元论哲学表达出来的那一种，其所构想出的洞见，乃是人类迄今达到的最高成就。”

我只好附和惠斯勒（Whistler）某天站在一幅糟糕透顶的素描前说的那句话：“上帝保佑我的灵魂”——说得又慢又深沉，然后又重复了一遍。

妙就妙在，主编还一本正经地加了条脚注，声明自己与此无涉；而我把这部大作拿给一位甲虫迷看，他眉头紧锁地读完，说道：“我看这东西登在这类刊物里，未免不太得体。”

（好一幅群像。）

10月12日
流感病倒了。

10月13日
昨夜齐柏林飞艇空袭。我正发着烧，但我们这个小家依旧镇定自若，感谢上帝。我们听见炮声阵阵，我躺在床上直打哆嗦。许多人因惊悸而心力衰竭死掉。

10月14日
仍卧床。昨夜无空袭。星期三响过两次警报，一次在9点半，一次在午夜。头一回，公寓管理员惊慌失措地跑上来说“齐柏林来了”，我们便熄了灯。随后午夜时分，趁别人都已睡熟，我听见街上一条大嗓门朝上喊：“那边的灯灭了。它们又来了。”我静静躺在床上等着。远处炮声隐隐。

10月17日
心脏病严重发作。

10月18日
心脏间歇。每三四分钟停一次。M——说我到这会儿早该习惯了！唉！！神经极度衰弱，胆小怕事。大剂量服用士的宁。但愿亲爱的E——别染上流感。她满怀希望地吞着奎宁。

10月19日
住在R——家。南下旅途糟透了，在克拉珀姆枢纽站和克罗伊登各换了一次车，心脏无时无刻不在间歇，什么姿势都一样。可怜的E——陪着我。今天惊觉自己居然还活着。

10月20日
今天好些了。费了好多口舌，总算劝得E——同意把公寓租出去，好让我们搬到齐柏林空袭圈外的乡下去。

10月24日
又回伦敦了。靠砒霜和士的宁撑着，人见好。神经兴奋过度，什么事也做不成。

10月25日
公寓出租的事如今交在中介手里，可怜的E——已完全听天由命，准备舍弃她那些心爱的墙纸等等了。

11月7日
公寓租出去了，我们现在住在————的寓所里，在伦敦以西20英里处。

11月8日

===Original===

It is a great relief to be down in the country. Zeppelins terrify me. Have just had a delightful experience in reading Conrad’s new book, *Victory*﻿—a welcome relief from all the tension of the past two months. To outward view, I have been merely a youth getting married, catching flu and giving up a London flat.

Inwardly, I have been whizzing around like a Catherine Wheel. Consider the items:

-

Concussion of the spine.

-

Resulting paralysis of left leg ten days before marriage.

-

Zeppelin raid (heard a cannon go off for the first time).

-

Severe cold in the head day before marriage (and therefore wild anxiety).

-

Successful marriage with abatement of cold.

-

Return to our home.

-

Ten days later, down with influenza.

-

A second zeppelin raid.

-

Bad heart attack.

-

Then flat sublet and London evacuated.

The record nauseates me. I am nauseated with myself and my self-centredness.﻿ ﻿… Suppose I have been “whizzing” as I call it﻿—what then? They are but subjective trifles﻿—meanwhile other men are seeing great adventures in Gallipoli and elsewhere. “The *Triumph* is gone,” exclaimed the Admiral who in a little group of naval officers on board the flagship had been watching H.M.S. *Triumph* sink in the Aegean. He shuts his telescope with a click and returns in great dudgeon to his own quarters. How I envy all these men who are participating in this War﻿—soldiers, sailors, war correspondents﻿—all who live and throb and are not afraid. I am a timid youth, anaemic, wear spectacles, and am frightened by a zep raid! How humiliating. I hate myself for a white-livered craven: I am suffocated for want of more life and courage. My damnable body is slowly killing off all my spirit and buoyancy. Even my mind is becoming blurred. My memory is like an old man’s exactly. (Ask —﻿—﻿—.)

---

===Chinese===

来到乡下，真是如释重负。齐柏林飞艇让我胆战心惊。刚读完康拉德（Conrad）的新书《胜利》（*Victory*），十分惬意——过去两个月的紧张总算得以纾解。从外表看，我不过是个结了婚、染上流感、退掉伦敦公寓的年轻人。

而在内里，我一直像一支转轮烟花似的团团乱转。且看细目：

-

脊椎震荡。

-

婚礼前十天，左腿因此瘫痪。

-

齐柏林空袭（生平头一回听见炮响）。

-

婚礼前一天重感冒（因而焦虑若狂）。

-

婚礼顺利，感冒渐愈。

-

回到我们的家。

-

十天后，流感病倒。

-

第二次齐柏林空袭。

-

心脏病严重发作。

-

随后公寓转租，撤离伦敦。

这份记录让我作呕。我恶心自己，恶心自己的自我中心。……就算我如自己所说的一直在“团团转”——那又怎样？这些不过是主观的琐屑——与此同时，别的男人正在加里波利等地亲历伟大的冒险。“‘凯旋号’沉了，”那位海军上将惊呼道——他当时正在旗舰上，与一小群海军军官一同目送“陛下舰凯旋号”（H.M.S. *Triumph*）沉入爱琴海。他“咔哒”一声收起望远镜，怒气冲冲地回自己舱里去了。我多么羡慕所有正投身这场战争的人——士兵、水手、随军记者——所有这些活着、热血奔涌、无所畏惧的人。而我是个怯懦的青年，贫血，戴眼镜，还被一次飞艇空袭吓得魂不附体！何其丢人。我恨自己是个没胆的懦夫：我因缺少更多的生命与勇气而窒闷。我这该死的躯体正一点点扼杀我全部的精神与生气。连我的头脑也日渐模糊。我的记性活脱脱像个老头儿。（问问————就知道。）

---

===Original===

Yet through all my nausea, here I remain happy to discuss myself and my little mishaps. I’m damned sick of myself and all my neurotic whimperings, and so I hereby and now intend to lead a new life and throw this Journal to the Devil. I want to mangle it, tear it to shreds. You smug, hypocritical readers! you’ll get no more of me. All you say I know is true before you say it and I know *now* all the criticism you are going to launch. So please spare yourself the trouble. You cannot enlighten me upon myself. I *know*. I disgust myself﻿—and you, and as for you, you can go to the Devil with this Journal.

Finis

November 27

Today, armed with a certificate from my Doctor in a sealed envelope and addressed “to the Medical Officer examining Mr. W. N. P. Barbellion,” I got leave to attend the recruiting office and offer my services to my King and Country. At the time, the fact that the envelope was sealed caused no suspicion and I had been comfortably carrying the document about in my pocket for days past.

Of course I attended merely as a matter of form under pressure of the authorities, as I knew I was totally unfit﻿—but not quite *how* unfit. After receiving this precious certificate, I learnt that K﻿—﻿— was recruiting Doctor at W﻿—﻿—, and he offered to “put me through in five minutes,” as he knows the state of my health. So at a time agreed upon, I went today and was immediately rejected as soon as he had stethoscoped my heart. The certificate therefore was not needed, and coming home in the train I opened it out of curiosity.﻿ ﻿…

I was quite casual and thought it would be merely interesting to see what M﻿—﻿— said.

It was.

“Some 18 months ago,” it ran, “Mr. Barbellion showed the just visible symptoms of —﻿—﻿— —﻿—﻿—”﻿—and although this fact was at once communicated to my relatives it was withheld from me and M﻿—﻿— therefore asked the M.O. to respect this confidence and to reject me without stating on what grounds. He went on to refer to my patellar and plantar reflexes, by which time I had had enough, tore the paper up and flung it out of the railway carriage window.

===Chinese===

然而作呕归作呕，我照旧兴致勃勃地在这儿谈论我自己和我这点小灾小难。我他妈的受够了自己，受够了自己全部神经质的哀鸣，所以我在此宣布：从现在起我要开始新生活，把这本日记扔给魔鬼。我要把它撕个稀烂，碎成万片。你们这些自鸣得意、假仁假义的读者！你们再也别想从我这里得到什么。你们要说的话，还没出口我早知道是对的；你们将要发动的全部批评，我*现在*就已一清二楚。所以请免开尊口，省省力气吧。你们点不亮我。我*知道*。我恶心我自己——也恶心你们；至于你们，尽可以带上这本日记一起见鬼去。

终

11月27日

今天，我获准前往征兵处，为国王和祖国效力，手里揣着医生（the Doctor）开具的证明——装在一只封了口的信封里，封面写着“致为W. N. P. 巴贝利恩（W. N. P. Barbellion）先生体检的军医官”。当时，信封是封着的这一点并未引起我的疑心，此前好几天，我一直心安理得地把它揣在口袋里四处走动。

当然，我前去不过是迫于官府之命、走个过场，因为我知道自己完全不合格——只是不知道*究竟*不合格到什么地步。拿到这份珍贵的证明之后，我得知K——是W——的征兵军医，他主动提出“五分钟就把我办过去”，因为他了解我的健康状况。于是按约定的时间，我今天去了，他用听诊器一听我的心脏，立刻把我刷了下来。证明因此没用上，坐火车回家的路上，我出于好奇把它拆开了。……

我当时满不在乎，心想看看M——写了些什么，不过图个有趣罢了。

确实有趣。

“约18个月前，”证明上写道，“巴贝利恩先生已显出————、————刚刚可辨的症状”——这件事虽然当即告知了我的亲属，却对我瞒了下来，因此M——请军医官尊重这一秘密，不必说明理由便将我淘汰。他接着又写到我的膝反射与足底反射，看到这里我受够了，把纸撕碎，扔出了火车车窗。

===Original===

I then returned to the Museum intending to find out what —﻿—﻿— —﻿—﻿— was in Clifford Allbutt’s *System of Medicine*. I wondered whether it was brain or heart; and the very thought gave me palpitation. I hope it is heart﻿—something short and sharp rather than lingering. But I believe it must be —﻿—﻿— of the brain, the opposite process of softening occurring in old age. I recall M﻿—﻿—’s words to me before getting married: that I had this “nerve weakness,” but I was more likely to succumb to pneumonia than to any nervous trouble, and that only twelve months’ happiness would be worth while.

---

On the whole I am amazed at the calm way in which I take this news. I was a fool never to have suspected serious nerve trouble before. Does dear E﻿—﻿— know? What did M﻿—﻿— tell her when he saw her before our marriage?

November 28

As soon as I woke up in this clear, country air this morning, I thought: —﻿—﻿— —﻿—﻿—. I have decided never to find out what it is. I shall find out in good time by the course of events.

A few years ago, the news would have scared me. But not so now. It only interests me. I have been happy, merry, and quite high-spirited today.

December 5

I believe it’s creeping paralysis. My left leg goes lame after a short walk. Fortunately E﻿—﻿— does not take alarm.

December 17

Spent the last two days, both of us, in a state of unrelieved gloom. The clouds never lifted for a moment﻿—it’s awful. I scarcely have spoken a word.﻿ ﻿… And eugenically, what kind of an infant would even a Mark Tapley expect of a father with a medical history like mine, and a mother with a nervous system like hers?﻿ ﻿… Could anything be more unfortunate? And the War? What may not have happened by this time next year? My health is grotesque.

December 20

I wonder if she knows. I believe she does but I am afraid to broach the matter in case she doesn’t. I think she must know something otherwise she would show more alarm over my leg, and when I went to the Recruiting Office she seemed to show no fear whatever lest they took me. Several times a day in the middle of a talk, or a meal, or a kiss, this problem flashes through my mind. I look at her but find no solution. However﻿—for the present﻿—the matter is not urgent.

1916

February 1

===Chinese===

随后我回到大英博物馆（the British Museum），打算在克利福德·奥尔巴特（Clifford Allbutt）的《医学体系》（*System of Medicine*）里查个明白，————、————究竟是什么。我拿不准是脑子还是心脏；单是这个念头就让我心悸。我希望是心脏——来个干脆利落的，总好过缠绵病榻。但我相信那必是脑子的————，恰好是老年出现的脑软化过程的反面。我想起M——婚前对我说过的话：我有这“神经衰弱”的毛病，但比起任何神经疾患，我更可能死于肺炎，还说十二个月的幸福就算值了。

---

总的说来，我自己都惊讶，接到这个消息我竟如此平静。我从前竟从未疑心自己有严重的神经疾病，真是个傻瓜。亲爱的E——知道吗？M——婚前见她时，对她说了什么？

11月28日

今晨一醒，在这清冽的乡间空气里，我头一个念头便是：————、————。我已打定主意，永远不去查明它是什么。时机到了，事态自会告诉我。

换作几年前，这消息会吓坏我。如今却不会。它只让我觉得有趣。今天我一整天都快活、欢畅、兴致高昂。

12月5日

我相信它就是“渐进性麻痹”。走一小段路，我的左腿就发瘸。所幸E——并不惊慌。

12月17日

过去两天，我们俩都沉浸在一种驱不散的阴郁里。乌云一刻也不曾散开——可怕极了。我几乎没说过一句话。……再从优生学的角度看：凭我这样的病史做父亲，凭她那样的神经系统做母亲，哪怕马克·塔普利（Mark Tapley）再世，还能指望生出什么样的婴儿？……还有比这更不幸的吗？还有这场战争呢？到明年今日，天知道又要出什么事？我的健康荒唐透顶。

12月20日

不知她是否知情。我相信她知道，可我又怕万一她不知道，不敢启齿。我想她多少必定知道些，否则见我的腿她该更惊慌些，而我去征兵处那天，她似乎全无生怕他们收下我的惶恐。一天里总有几回，正说着话、吃着饭，或吻着吻着，这个问题倏地掠过心头。我望着她，却寻不到答案。不过——眼下——此事还不算紧迫。

1916年

2月1日

===Original===

Since I last wrote﻿—a month ago﻿—I have recovered my buoyancy after a blow which kept me under water so long I thought I should never come up and be happy again.﻿ ﻿… I was reciting my woes to R﻿—﻿—, and gaining much relief thereby, when we espied another crony on the other side of the street, crossed over at once, bandied words with him and then walked on, picking up the thread of my lugubrious story just where I had left off﻿—secretly staggered at my emotional agility. I’ve got to this now﻿—I simply don’t care.

February 2

“And she draiglet all her petticoatie, Coming through the rye.” These words have a ridiculous fascination for me; I cannot resist their saccharine, affectionate, nay amorous jingle and keep repeating them aloud all over the house﻿—as Lamb once kept reciting “Rose Aylmer.”

February 16

We took possession of our country cottage today: very charming and overlooking a beautiful Park.

Have just discovered the *Journal of the de Goncourts* and been reading it greedily. Life has really been a commodity. I am boiling over with vitality, chattering amiably to everyone about nothing﻿—argumentative, sanguine, serious, ridiculous. I called old R﻿—﻿— a Rapscallion, a Curmudgeon, and a Scaramouche, and E﻿—﻿— a trull, a drab, a trollop, a callet. “You certainly are a unique husband,” said that sweet little lady, and I.﻿ ﻿…

With me, one of the symptoms of delirium is always a melodramatic truculence! I shake my fist in R﻿—﻿—’s face and make him explode with laughing.﻿ ﻿… The sun today, and the great, whopping white clouds all bellied out, made me feel inside quite a bright young dog wriggling its body in ecstatic delight let loose upon the green sward.

“You must come down for a weekend,” I said to R﻿—﻿— at lunch. “Come down as soon as you can. You will find every comfort. It is an enormous house﻿—I have not succeeded in finding my way about it and﻿—it’s dangerous to lose yourself﻿—makes you late for dinner. When you arrive our gilded janitor will say: ‘I believe Mr. Barbellion is in the library.’ ”

“Black eunuchs wait on you at dinner, I suppose,” R﻿—﻿— rejoined.

“Oh! yes and golden chandeliers and a marble staircase﻿—all in barbaric splendour.”

“Yes, I shall certainly be glad to come down,” said R﻿—﻿—, phlegmatically.

===Chinese===

自上次提笔——已是一个月前——我又恢复了心气的轻快。此前那记打击把我压在水底那么久，我以为自己再也浮不上来、再也不会快乐了。……当时我正向R——数说自己的倒霉事，正数得心里松快，忽见街对面又来了一位老友，便立刻穿街过去，同他寒暄几句，随后接着往前走，把我那哀伤的故事从原先中断的地方原样续上——心里暗暗惊异于自己情绪上的敏捷。我如今到了这步田地——干脆满不在乎了。

2月2日

“她拖着湿漉漉的衬裙，一路从黑麦田里走来。”这两句诗对我有种荒唐的魔力；我抗拒不了那甜腻、亲昵、简直是多情的韵脚，满屋子大声念个不停——就像兰姆（Lamb）当年反复吟诵《罗斯·艾尔默》（“Rose Aylmer”）一样。

2月16日

今天我们住进了乡间小屋：十分可爱，俯瞰着一座美丽的园子。

刚发现龚古尔兄弟的《日记》（*Journal of the de Goncourts*），正贪婪地读着。生命当真又成了件好东西。我浑身活力四溢，逢人便亲亲热热地闲扯——好争辩，乐天，一本正经，又滑稽可笑。我骂老R——是无赖、老吝啬鬼、招摇撞骗的丑角，骂E——是荡妇、贱妇、懒婆娘、小骚货。“你可真是个天下独一份的丈夫，”那位可爱的小夫人说道，而我呢。……

在我身上，谵妄的症状之一永远是一种戏台上的凶横！我朝R——挥拳头，惹得他放声大笑。……今天的太阳，还有那些鼓着白肚皮的大团云朵，让我打心底里觉得自己像条撒欢的小狗，被撒到绿茵草地上，快活得直打滚。

“你一定得来过一个周末，”午饭时我对R——说。“尽早来。样样舒适。那是座大宅子——我自己都还没摸清方向，而且——迷起路来可不是玩的——会误了晚饭。你一到，我们那位镀金的门房就会说：‘我想巴贝利恩先生在书房里。’”

“开饭时还有黑太监伺候吧，我猜，”R——应道。

“哦！有，还有金色枝形吊灯和大理石楼梯——一派蛮邦的豪奢。”

“好，我一定高兴来住，”R——说道，不动声色。

===Original===

And so on and so on. Words, idle words all day in a continuous rush. And I am sure that the match which fired the gunpowder was the discovery of the de Goncourts’ Journal! It’s extraordinary how I have been going on from week to week quite calmly for all the world as if I had read all the books and seen all the places and done everything according to the heart’s desire. This book has really jolted me out of my complacency: to think that all this time, I have been dead to so much! Why I might have died unconscious that the de Goncourts had ever lived and written their colossal book and now I am aware of it, I am all in a fever to read it and take it up into my brain: I might die now before I have finished it﻿—a thought that makes me wild with desire just as I once endured most awful pangs when I felt my health going, and believed that I might die before having ever been in love﻿—to die and never to have been in love!﻿—for an instant at a time this possibility used to make me writhe.

March 22

R﻿—﻿— has an unpleasant habit of making some scarifying announcement drawing forth an explosive query from me and then lapsing at once into an Eleusinian silence: he appears to take a sensuous pleasure in the pause that keeps you expectant. I could forgive a man who keeps you on tenterhooks for two puffs in order to keep his pipe alight, but R﻿—﻿— shuts up out of sheer self-indulgence and goes on gazing at the horizon with the eyes of a seer (he thinks) trying to cod me he sees a portent there only revealed to God’s elect.

I told him this in the middle of one of his luxurious silences. “I will tell you,” he said deliberately, “when we reach the Oratory.” (We were in Brompton Road.)

“Which side of it?” I enquired anxiously. “This or that?”

“That,” said he, “will depend on how you behave in the meantime.”

April 3

We met a remarkable Bulldog today in the street, humbly following behind a tiny boy to whom it was attached by a piece of string. At the time we were following in the wake of three magnificent Serbian Officers, and I was particularly interesting myself in the curious cut of their top boots. But the Bulldog was the Red Herring in our path.

“Is that a Dog?” I asked the little boy.

===Chinese===

如此这般，不一而足。一整天闲话连篇，滔滔不断。而我确信，点燃这桶火药的那粒火星，正是龚古尔兄弟《日记》的发现！说来奇怪，一周又一周，我竟能这样安之若素地过日子，俨然读遍了天下书、看遍了天下景、桩桩件件都遂了心愿似的。这本书当真把我从自满里摇醒了：想想看，这许多时日，我竟对这么多东西浑噩无知！我差一点儿到死都不知道龚古尔兄弟其人曾在世上活过、写过他们那部皇皇巨著；如今既已知晓，我便心热难耐，非要把它读完、装进脑子不可：万一没读完我就死了呢——这个念头撩得我欲火中烧，正如当年我觉出健康日渐崩坏、相信自己可能到死都不曾恋爱过一样，曾叫我备受煎熬——死了，却从未爱过！——这个可能性曾一阵一阵搅得我翻腾。

3月22日

R——有个讨厌的习惯：冷不丁抛出一句吓人的话，引得我失声追问，他却随即陷入厄琉西斯式的沉默：那种吊人胃口的停顿，似乎能给他带来感官的享受。一个人为了不让烟斗熄灭、喷两口烟的工夫吊着你，这我可以原谅；可R——闭口不言纯粹出于自我陶醉，还继续用先知般的眼睛（他自己这么以为）凝望地平线，想诓我相信他在那儿看见了只向上帝选民揭示的凶兆。

我趁着他一次酣畅的沉默，把这话直说了。“等到了祈祷所，”他慢条斯理地说，“我自会告诉你。”（我们当时在布朗普顿路。）

“在哪一边？”我急切地追问。“这边还是那边？”

“那个嘛，”他说，“就看你这期间表现如何了。”

4月3日

今天在街上遇见一只了不得的斗牛犬，谦卑地跟在一个小不点儿男孩身后，被一根细绳拴着。当时我们正尾随三位气宇轩昂的塞尔维亚军官，我尤其对他们的长筒马靴奇特的剪裁兴味盎然。可这只斗牛犬偏偏横在路上，成了岔开我们注意力的障眼法。

“那是一只狗吗？”我问那小男孩。

===Original===

He assured me that it was, and so it turned out to be, though Bullfrog would have been a better name for it, the forelegs being more bandied, the back broader and the mouth wider than in any Bulldog I have ever seen. It was a super-Bulldog.

We turned and walked on. “There,” said R﻿—﻿—, “now we have lost our Serbian Officers.”

April 4

“May I use your microscope?” he asked.

“By all means,” I said with a gesture of elaborate politeness.

He sat down at my table, in my chair, and used my instrument﻿—becoming at once absorbed and oblivious to my banter as per below:

“As Scotchmen,” I said, “are monuments rather than men, this latest raid on Edinboro’s worthy inhabitants must be called vandalism rather than murder.”

No answer. I continued to stand by my chair.

“How pleased Swift, Johnson, Lamb, and other anti-Caledonians would be.﻿ ﻿…”

“Hope you don’t mind my occupying your chair a little longer,” the Scotchman said, “but this is a larva, has curious maxillae.﻿ ﻿…” and his voice faded away in abstraction.

“Oh! no﻿—go on,” I said, “I fear it is a grievous absence of hospitality on my part in not providing you with a glass of whiskey. Can I offer you water, Sir?”

No answer.

Another enthusiast ushered himself in, was greeted with delight by the first and invited to sit down. I pulled out a chair for him and said:

“Shave, sir, or hair cut?”

“If you follow along to the top of the galea,” No. I droned on imperturbably, “you will.﻿ ﻿…” etc.

I got tired of standing and talking to an empty house but at last they got up, apologising and making for the door.

I entreated them not to mention the matter﻿—my fee should be nominal﻿—I did it out of sheer love, etc.

They thanked me again and would have said more but I added blandly:

“You know your way out?” They assured me they did (having worked in the place for 30 years and more)﻿—I thanked God﻿—and sat down to my table once more.

(These reports of conversations are rather fatuous: yet they give an idea of the sort of person I have to deal with, and also the sort of person I am among this sort of person.)

April 6

The Housefly Problem﻿—1916

===Chinese===

他向我保证是狗，后来证明确实是——只是叫它“牛蛙”更贴切：那前腿外弯得更厉害，背更宽，嘴比我见过的任何斗牛犬都阔。那是一只超级斗牛犬。

我们转身继续走。“瞧，”R——说，“这下咱们的塞尔维亚军官丢了。”

4月4日

“我能用用你的显微镜吗？”他问。

“请便，”我说，还郑重其事地做了个优雅的手势。

他在我的桌子旁、我的椅子上坐下，用起我的仪器来——立刻全神贯注，对我以下的打趣充耳不闻：

“既然苏格兰人，”我说，“与其说是活人，不如说是纪念碑，那么最近这次对爱丁堡可敬市民的空袭，就该称作破坏文物，而非谋害人命了。”

没有回答。我仍站在我的椅子旁边。

“斯威夫特、约翰逊、兰姆，还有其他那些反苏格兰分子该多高兴啊。……”

“希望您不介意我多占用一会儿您的椅子，”那位苏格兰人说，“这是只幼虫，上颚很奇特……”他的声音在出神中渐渐隐去。

“哦！不妨事——接着看，”我说，“只怕是我失了待客之道，没给您备上一杯威士忌，深以为憾。给您来杯水如何，先生？”

没有回答。

又一位热心人自己走了进来，头一位欢天喜地地迎上去，请他坐下。我为他拉出一把椅子，说：

“先生，刮脸还是理发？”

“要是您顺着一路看到兜帽顶端，”一号先生不动声色地继续念叨，“您就会……”云云。

我站累了，也倦于对着一屋子空气回答，好歹他们终于起身，道着歉朝门口走去。

我恳请他们别把这事放在心上——费用仅具象征意义——我是纯粹出于爱好才做这个的，等等。

他们再次道谢，还想多说几句，我却又和颜悦色地补上一句：

“认得出去的路吧？”他们向我保证认得（毕竟在这地方干了三十多年）——我谢天谢地——重新在我的桌前坐下。

（这些对话记录相当愚憨：不过它们多少让人看出，我不得不打交道的都是些什么人，也看出我在这些人中间又是个什么人。）

4月6日

家蝇问题——1916

===Original===

For weeks past we have all been in a terrible flutter scarcely paralleled by the outbreak of Armageddon in August, 1914. The spark which fired almost the whole building was a letter to the *Times* written by Dr. —﻿—﻿—, making public an ignominious confession of ignorance on the part of Entomologists as to how the Housefly passed the winter. In reply, many correspondents wrote to say they hibernated, and one man was even so temerarious as to quote to us Entomologists the exact Latin name of the Housefly: viz., *Musca domestica*. We asked for specimens and enormous numbers of flies at once began to arrive at the Museum, alive and dead﻿—and not a Housefly among them! So there was a terrible howdedo.

One of the correspondents was named “Masefield.” “Not Masefield the poet?” an excited dipterist asked. I reassured him.

“I’ve a good mind,” said Dr. —﻿—﻿—, “to reply to this chap who’s so emphatic and give him a whigging﻿—only he’s climbing down a bit in this second letter in today’s issue.” I strongly advocated clemency.

But still the affair goes on. Every morning sees more letters and more flies sent by all sorts of persons﻿—we seem to have set the whole world searching for Houseflies﻿—Duchesses, signalmen, farmers, footmen. Every morning each fresh batch of flies is mounted on pins by experts in the Setting Room, and an Assistant’s whole time is devoted to identifying, arranging, listing and reporting upon the new arrivals. At the last meeting of the Trustees a sample collection was displayed to show indubitably that the insects which hibernate in houses are not *Musca domestica* but *Pollenia rudis*. I understand the Trustees were appreciative.

===Chinese===

几个星期以来，我们全体上下一直陷于一场可怕的骚动，几乎堪与1914年8月末日大战爆发时的情景相比。点燃了差不多整座大楼的火星，是————博士投给《泰晤士报》（*Times*）的一封信，信中公开承认了昆虫学家们一桩丢脸的无知：家蝇究竟如何过冬，我们竟不知道。作为回应，许多来信人写信说家蝇是冬眠过冬的，还有一位胆大包天，竟向我们昆虫学家援引家蝇的准确拉丁学名：即*Musca domestica*（家蝇）。我们请求寄来标本，于是大批大批的苍蝇立刻开始源源抵达博物馆，死的活的都有——中间竟没有一只是家蝇！于是一场鸡飞狗跳的大乱子。

有位来信人名叫“梅斯菲尔德”。“该不会是诗人梅斯菲尔德吧？”一位激动的双翅目学者问。我让他放心。

“我真想，”————博士说，“回信教训教训这个口气强硬的家伙，好好训他一顿——只是今天这期里他的第二封信，语气已经软了点。”我力主宽大为怀。

可事情仍没完没了。每天早晨都有更多信件、更多苍蝇从形形色色的人手里寄来——我们似乎是发动了全世界搜捕家蝇——公爵夫人、信号员、农夫、听差，无一例外。每天早晨，新到的一批批苍蝇由展翅室的专家们钉上昆虫针，一名助理的全部时间都用来鉴定、整理、编目并报告新来者。上一次馆务会议上展出一批样本，确凿表明在住宅里越冬的昆虫并非*Musca domestica*，而是*Pollenia rudis*（毛蝇）。据我所知，诸位理事颇为满意。

===Original===

An observant eye can now discover state visits to our dipterists from interested persons carrying their flies with them, animated discussions in the corridor, knots of excited enthusiasts in the Lavatory, in the Library, everywhere﻿—and everywhere the subject discussed is the same: How does the Housefly pass the winter? As one passes one catches: “In Bakehouses certainly they are to be found but.﻿ ﻿…” or a wistful voice, “I wish I had caught that one in my bathroom three winters ago﻿—I am certain it was a Housefly.” The Doctor himself﻿—a gallant Captain﻿—wanders from room to room stimulating his lieutenants to make suggestions, and examining every answer to the great interrogative on its merits, no matter how humble or insignificant the person who makes it. Then of an afternoon he will entirely disappear, and word goes round that he has set forth to examine a rubbish heap in Soho or Pimlico. As the afternoon draws to its close someone enquires if he has come back yet; next morning a second asks if I had seen him, then a third announces mournfully that he has just been holding conversation with him, but that nothing at all was found in the rubbish heap.

The great sensation of all occurred last week when somebody ran along the corridor crying that Mr. —﻿—﻿— had just found a Housefly in his room. We were all soon agog with the news, and the excited Captain was presently espied setting out for the scene of operations with a killing bottle and net. The insect was promptly impounded and identified as a veritable *Musca domestica*. A consultation being held to sit on the body, a lady finally laid information that two “forced Houseflies” hatched the day before had escaped from her possession. She suggested Mr. —﻿—﻿—’s specimen was one of them.

“How would it get from your room to Mr. —﻿—﻿—’s?” she was immediately asked. And breathless, we all heard her answer deliberately and quite audibly that the fugitive may have gone out of her window, up the garden and in by Mr. —﻿—﻿—’s window, *or* it may have gone out of her door, up the corridor and in by his door. I wanted to know why it should have entered Mr. —﻿—﻿—’s room as he is not a dipterist but a microlepidopterist. They looked at me sternly and we slowly dispersed.

===Chinese===

如今只要留心，就能看见关心此事的人带着各自的苍蝇，对我们双翅目学者一一作正式拜访，走廊里争得热火朝天，盥洗室里、图书馆里，处处是一团团兴奋的热心人——而无论何处，谈论的都是同一个题目：家蝇是怎么过冬的？路过时总能听见：“面包房里肯定找得到它们，不过……”或是一个怅惘的声音：“三年前冬天在我浴室里见到的要是逮住就好了——我敢肯定那是一只家蝇。”医生本尊——一位英武的上尉——挨个房间巡视，激励他的部下们献计献策，对这一大问号的每一个回答都按其本身的价值加以审察，无论提议者多么无足轻重。然后，某天下午他会彻底消失，有话传来，说他出发去察看苏活区或皮姆利科的一处垃圾堆了。临近傍晚，有人问回来了没有；次日清晨，第二个人问我见他没有；接着第三个人哀伤地宣布，方才还同他说过话来着，只是那垃圾堆里一无所获。

最大的轰动发生在上星期，有人沿着走廊一路喊着说，————先生刚在自己的房间里发现了一只家蝇。消息一出，人人翘首，很快便望见那位激动的上尉带着毒瓶和捕网奔赴现场。那只昆虫当即被没收充公，鉴定为一只如假包换的*Musca domestica*。随即召开“验尸”会议，最后有位女士检举说，前一天孵出的两只“人工催育家蝇”从她手里逃走了。她提出，————先生那只标本就是其中之一。

“那它怎么从你的房间跑到————先生房间里去的呢？”马上有人问她。我们屏住呼吸，听她从容而清晰地答道：那逃犯可能是从她的窗户出去，沿花园上去，从————先生的窗户进去的，*也*可能是从她的房门出去，沿走廊上去，从他的房门进去的。我想知道它为什么偏要进————先生的房间，因为他并非双翅目学者，而是微小鳞翅目学者。他们严厉地瞪了我一眼，我们便慢慢散去了。

===Original===

This morning, the Dr. came to me with a newspaper cutting in his hand, saying, “*The Times* is behindhand.” He handed me the slip. It was a clipping from today’s *Times* about a sackful of flies which had been taken from Wandsworth Clock Tower in a state of hibernation.

“Behindhand?” I asked timidly, for I felt that all the story was not in front of me.

“Why, yes. Don’t you know?”

I knew nothing, but was prepared for anything.

“*The Star*, two days ago,” he informed me, “had a paragraph about this﻿—headed ‘*Tempus fugit*’ ”﻿—this last in a resentful tone as though the frivolous reporter were attempting to discredit our mystery.

There was a long pause. Neither of us spoke. Then he slowly said:

“I wonder why *The Times* is so behindhand. This is two days late.”

May 5

Hulloa, old friend: how are you? I mean my Diary. I haven’t written to you for ever so long, and my silence as usual indicates happiness. I have been passing through an unbroken succession of calm happy days, walking in the woods with my darling, or doing a little gentle gardening on coming home in the evening﻿—and the War has been centuries away. Later on towards bedtime, E﻿—﻿— reads Richard Jefferies, I play Patience and Mrs. —﻿—﻿— makes garments for Priscilla.

The only troubles have been a chimney which smokes and a neighbour’s dog which barks at night. So to be sure, I have made port after storm at last﻿—and none too soon. Today my cheerfulness has been rising in a crescendo till tonight it broke in such a handsome crest of pure delight that I cannot think of going to bed without recording it.

Pachmann

After sitting on the wall around the fountain in the middle of Trafalgar Square, eating my sandwiches and feeding the Pigeons with the crumbs, I listened for a moment to the roar of the traffic around three sides of the Square as I stood in the centre quite alone, what time one fat old pigeon, all unconcerned, was treading another. It was an extraordinary experience: motor horns tooted incessantly and it seemed purposelessly, so that one had the fancy that all London was out for a joyride﻿—it was a great British Victory perhaps, or Peace Day.

===Chinese===

今天早晨，医生手里拿着一份剪报走到我跟前，说：“《泰晤士报》落后了。”他把那纸条递给我。那是今天《泰晤士报》上的一则剪报，说的是从温兹沃思钟楼里取出的一麻袋处于冬眠状态的苍蝇。

“落后了？”我怯怯地问，因为我感到事情的原委还不全在我眼前。

“嗐，可不是。你不知道？”

我一无所知，但对什么都做好了准备。

“两天前的《星报》（*The Star*），”他告知我，“登了这么一段——标题是‘光阴飞逝’（*Tempus fugit*）”——最后这几个字带着忿忿的口气，仿佛那轻浮的记者企图玷污我们的神秘事件似的。

长长的沉默。我们谁也没说话。然后他缓缓说道：

“真不明白《泰晤士报》为什么这么迟钝。这都晚了两天了。”

5月5日

你好啊，老朋友：近来可好？我说的是我的日记。好久好久没给你写信了，而我的沉默一如既往，意味着幸福。我一直过着一段连绵不断的平静快乐的日子，同我的心肝一起在林间散步，傍晚回到家摆弄一会儿花草——战争已是几个世纪之外的事了。快到就寝时分，E——读理查德·杰弗里斯（Richard Jefferies），我玩单人纸牌，————太太在给普丽西拉（Priscilla）缝衣裳。

唯一的烦恼不过是一口倒烟的烟囱和一条夜里吠叫的邻家狗。这么着，历经风浪，我总算进了港——而且来得不算早。今天我的快活劲儿一波高过一波，到夜里终于涌成一道纯粹欢愉的漂亮浪峰，不记上一笔便无法上床安睡。

帕赫曼

在特拉法加广场中央喷泉四周的矮墙上坐过，吃了三明治，把碎屑喂了鸽子之后，我独自一人站在广场正中，听了一会儿三面环绕的车马喧嚣，与此同时，一只肥老的鸽子正若无其事地踩着另一只交尾。那是一次奇特的体验：汽车喇叭响个不停，似乎毫无来由，让人恍惚觉得全伦敦都出来兜风了——也许是一场大英帝国的大捷，或是停战纪念日。

===Original===

Then walked down Whitehall to Westminster Bridge in time to see the 2 o’clock boat start upstream for Kew. I loitered by the old fellow with the telescope who keeps his pitch by Boadicea: I saw a piper of the Scots Guards standing near gazing across the river but at nothing in particular﻿—just idling as I was. I saw another man sitting on the stone steps and reading a dirty fragment of newspaper. I saw the genial, red-faced seafaring man in charge of the landing stage strolling up and down his small domain﻿—chatting, jesting, spitting, and making fast a rope or so. Everything was *alive* to the finger tips, vividly shining, pulsating.

Arrived at Queen’s Hall in time for Pachmann’s Recital at 3:15.﻿ ﻿… As usual he kept us waiting for 10 minutes. Then a short, fat, middle-aged man strolled casually on to the platform and everyone clapped violently﻿—so it was Pachmann: a dirty greasy looking fellow with long hair of dirty grey colour, reaching down to his shoulders and an ugly face. He beamed on us and then shrugged his shoulders and went on shrugging them until his eye caught the music stool, which seemed to fill him with amazement. He stalked it carefully, held out one hand to it caressingly, and finding all was well, went two steps backwards, clasping his hands before him and always gazing at the little stool in mute admiration, his eyes sparkling with pleasure, like Mr. Pickwick’s on the discovery of the archeological treasure. He approached once more, bent down and ever so gently moved it about ⅞ths of an inch nearer the piano. He then gave it a final pat with his right hand and sat down.

He played Nocturne No. 2, Prelude No. 20, a Mazurka and two Etudes of Chopin and Schubert’s Impromptu No. 4.

At the close we all crowded around the platform and gave the queer, old-world gentleman an ovation, one man thrusting up his hand which Pachmann generously shook as desired.

===Chinese===

随后沿白厅街走到威斯敏斯特桥，正赶上两点钟开往上游邱园的班船启航。我在那位守着望远镜的老头儿身边流连，他的摊子就摆在博阿迪西亚雕像旁：我看见近旁站着一名苏格兰近卫军的风笛手，望着河对岸出神，却并没看什么特别的东西——同我一样只是闲晃。我看见另一个人坐在石阶上，读着一块脏兮兮的报纸残片。我看见那位红光满面、和蔼可亲的水手出身的浮码头管理人，在他那一小片领地里踱来踱去——聊天，说笑，吐痰，不时把一两条缆绳系牢。一切都*活*到了指尖，鲜亮夺目，脉搏翕张。

3点1刻赶到女王音乐厅，正赶上帕赫曼的独奏会。……他照例让我们等了十分钟。随后一个矮胖的中年男子慢悠悠地踱上台来，众人使劲鼓掌——原来这就是帕赫曼：一个看上去油腻邋遢的家伙，一头脏灰色的长发直披到肩，其貌不扬。他朝我们眉开眼笑，然后耸耸肩，耸个没完，直到目光落在琴凳上，那凳子似乎让他惊叹不已。他小心翼翼地向它潜近，伸出一只手爱抚它，确信一切无恙之后，退后两步，双手在胸前合十，始终默默景仰地凝视着那只小凳子，双眼喜得放光，就像匹克威克先生（Mr. Pickwick）发现考古珍宝时那样。他再度上前，弯下腰，无比轻柔地把凳子往钢琴挪近了约八分之七英寸。然后用右手最后拍它一下，坐了下来。

他弹了第二号夜曲、第二十号前奏曲、一首玛祖卡和肖邦的两首练习曲，还有舒伯特的第四号即兴曲。

终场时我们全拥到台前，向这位古怪的老派绅士欢呼致意，有一个人把手伸上去，帕赫曼大方便如其所愿地握了握。

===Original===

As an encore he gave us a Valse﻿—“Valse, Valse,” he exclaimed ecstatically, jumping up and down in his seat in time to the music. It was a truly remarkable sight: on his right the clamorous crowd around the platform; on his left the seat holders of the Orchestra Stalls, while at the piano bobbed this grubby little fat man playing divine Chopin divinely well, at the same time rising and falling in his seat, turning a beaming countenance first to the right and then to the left, crying, “Valse, Valse.” He is as entertaining as a tumbler at a variety hall.

As soon as he had finished, we clapped and rattled for more, Pachmann meanwhile standing surrounded by his idolaters in affected despair at ever being able to satisfy us. Presently he walked off and a scuffle was half visible behind the scenes between him and his agent who sent him in once more.

The applause was wonderful. As soon as he began again it ceased on the instant, and as soon as he left off it started again immediately﻿—nothing boisterous or rapturous but a steady, determined thunder of applause that came regularly and evenly like the roar from some machine.

May 20

Spent a quiet day. Sat at my escritoire in the Studio this morning writing an Essay, with a large 4-fold window on my left, looking on to woods and fields, with Linnets, Greenfinches, Cuckoos calling. This afternoon while E﻿—﻿— rested awhile I sat on the veranda in the sun and read *Antony and Cleopatra*.﻿ ﻿… Yes, I’m in harbour at last. I’d be the last to deny it but I cannot believe it will last. It’s too good to last and it’s all too good to be even true. E﻿—﻿— is too good to be true, the home is too good to be true, and this quiet restful existence is too wonderful to last in the middle of a great war. It’s just a little deceitful April sunshine, that’s all.﻿ ﻿…25

Had tea at the —﻿—﻿—. A brilliant summer’s evening. Afterwards, we wandered into the garden and shrubbery and sat about on the turf of the lawn, chatting and smoking. Mr. —﻿—﻿— played with a rogue of a white Tomcat called Chatham, and E﻿—﻿— talked about our neighbour, “Shamble legs,” about garden topics, etc. Then I strolled into the drawing-room where Cynthia was playing Chopin on a grand piano. Is it not all perfectly lovely?

===Chinese===

作为加演，他给我们弹了一首圆舞曲——“圆舞曲，圆舞曲，”他狂喜地喊着，在座椅上随着音乐一起一伏。那真是幅了不得的景象：他右边是台前喧嚷的人群；左边是正厅前排的持票看客，而钢琴前颠动着这个脏兮兮的小胖子，把神圣的肖邦弹得出神入化，一面在座椅上一起一伏，一面把一张喜洋洋的脸先转向右边再转向左边，口中喊着：“圆舞曲，圆舞曲。”他跟杂耍戏院里的翻筋斗艺人一样逗乐。

他一弹完，我们便拍着叫着还要再来，帕赫曼被他的崇拜者们团团围住，装出一副无论如何也满足不了我们的绝望神情。片刻他走进去了，幕后隐约可见他和经纪人推搡了一阵，经纪人又把他推了出来。

掌声妙不可言。他只要一开始弹，掌声顷刻止息；他一停手，掌声立刻又起——不是狂风暴雨式的喧腾，而是一阵沉稳、执着的雷鸣，规律而均匀，宛如某台机器的轰鸣。

5月20日

过了安静的一天。今晨坐在画室的书桌前写一篇随笔，左边是一扇四开的大窗，望得见树林与田野，朱雀、金翅雀、杜鹃声声啼啭。今天下午E——小憩时，我坐在游廊上晒太阳，读《安东尼与克莉奥佩特拉》（*Antony and Cleopatra*）。……是的，我总算进港停泊了。要我否认这个，我是最不肯的，可我不信这能长久。好得难以长久，一切都好得简直不像真的。E——好得不像真的，这个家好得不像真的，而在这场大战正酣之际，这样恬静安适的日子实在太美，美得难以持久。这不过是四月里一小片骗人的阳光罢了，仅此而已。……25

在————喝了茶。一个明媚的夏日傍晚。随后我们信步走进花园灌木丛，在草坪的草地上随处坐下，抽烟聊天。————先生逗弄着一只名叫“查塔姆”的捣蛋大白公猫，E——则谈起我们的邻居“蹒跚腿”，谈些园艺话题等等。然后我踱进客厅，辛西娅正在大钢琴上弹肖邦。这一切岂不美妙极了？

===Original===

How delicious to be silent, lolling on the Chesterfield, gazing abstractedly through the lattice window and listening to the lulling charities of Nocturne No. 2, Op. 37! The melody in the latter part of this nocturne took me back at once to a cloudless day in an open boat in the Bay of Combemartin, with oars up and the water quietly and regularly lapping the gunwales as we rose and fell. A state of the most profound calm and happiness took possession of me.

June 2

From the local paper:

“A comrade in the Gloucesters writing to a friend at —﻿—﻿— mentions that Pte. J﻿—﻿— has been fatally shot in action. J﻿—﻿— was well known here for years as an especially smart young news vendor.”

June 3

What a bitter disappointment it is to realise that people the most intimately in love with one another are really separated by such a distance. A woman is calmly knitting socks or playing Patience while her husband or sweetheart lies dead in Flanders. However strong the tie that binds them together yet they are insufficiently *en rapport* for her to sense even a catastrophe﻿—and she must wait till the War Office forsooth sends her word. How humiliating that the War Office must do what Love cannot. Human love seems then such a superficial thing. Every person is a distinct egocentric being. Each for himself and the Devil take the hindmost. “Ah! but she didn’t know.” “Yes, but she *ought* to have known.” Mental telepathy and clairvoyance should be common at least to all lovers.

---

This morning in bed I heard a man with a milkcart say in the road to a villager at about 6:30 a.m., “… battle﻿ ﻿… and we lost six cruisers.” This was the first I knew of the Battle of Jutland. At 8 a.m. I read in the *Daily News* that the British Navy had been defeated, and thought it was the end of all things. The news took away our appetites. At the railway station, the *Morning Post* was more cheerful, even reassuring, and now at 6:30 p.m. the Battle has turned into a merely regrettable indecisive action. We breathe once more.

June 4

It has now become a victory.

June 11

===Chinese===

斜倚在切斯特菲尔德长沙发上一言不发，心不在焉地望着格子窗，听第二号夜曲（作品第37号）那抚慰人心的慈悲，何等惬意！这首夜曲后半段的旋律，霎时把我带回科布马丁湾（Combemartin）一个无云的日子：敞着的小船上，桨已收起，船身随着波浪起伏，海水安详地、有节律地舐着船舷。一种至深的宁静与幸福攫住了我。

6月2日

摘自本地报纸：

“一名在格洛斯特郡团服役的战友，在写给————一位友人的信中提到，二等兵J——在战斗中中弹，伤重不治。多年来，J——在本地的报童里是出了名的精干小伙子。”

6月3日

最相亲相爱的人们，彼此间竟隔着如此遥远的距离——意识到这一点，是何等苦涩的失望。一个女人正安然织着袜子或玩着单人纸牌，而她的丈夫或情郎已陈尸法兰德斯。无论联结他们的纽带多么牢固，他们之间的*心灵相通*仍不足以让她哪怕感应到一场大难——她还非得等陆军部大发慈悲发来通知不可。陆军部得替爱情做爱情做不到的事，何其丢人。人间的爱看来是何等浅薄的东西。每个人都是各自封闭的自我中心体。人各为己，落后者遭殃。“啊！可她并不知道。”“是，可她*本该*知道。”心灵感应与天眼通，至少在一切恋人之间本该是寻常事。

---

今晨6点半光景，我在床上听见路上一个赶奶车的人对村里人说：“……海战……我们损失了六艘巡洋舰。”这是我对日德兰海战的最初所闻。8点钟，我在《每日新闻》（*Daily News*）上读到英国海军吃了败仗，心想这下万事皆休。这消息让我们茶饭无心。到了火车站，《晨邮报》（*Morning Post*）口气乐观了些，甚至令人宽慰；而到傍晚6点半，这场海战已变成一场仅仅令人遗憾的、胜负未分的交锋。我们又能喘气了。

6月4日

如今它已成了一场大捷。

6月11日

===Original===

Old systems of Classification: Rafinesc’s Theory of Fives, Swainson’s Theory of Sevens, Edward Newman’s book called *Sphinx Vespiformis* tracing fives throughout the animal world, Sir Thomas Browne’s Quincunx, chasing fives throughout the whole of nature﻿—in the words of Coleridge, “quincunxes in Heaven above, quincunxes in the Earth below, quincunxes in the mind of man, in optic nerves, in roots of trees, in leaves, in everything!”

Old false trails:

The Philosopher’s Stone (Balthazar Claes).26

A universal catholicon (Bishop Berkeley’s tar-water). Mystical numbers (as per above).

My father was Sir Thomas Browne and my mother Marie Bashkirtseff. See what a curious hybrid I am!

---

I toss these pages in the faces of timid, furtive, respectable people and say: “There! that’s me! You may like it or lump it, but it’s true. And I challenge you to follow suit, to flash the searchlight of your self-consciousness into every remotest corner of your life and invite everybody’s inspection. Be candid, be honest, break down the partitions of your cubicle, come out of your burrow, little worm.” As we are all such worms we should at least be honest worms.

---

My gratitude to E﻿—﻿— for plucking me out of the hideous miseries of my life in London is greater than I can express. If I were the cheap hero of a ladies’ novel I should immolate my journals as a token, and you would have a pretty picture of a pale young man watching his days go up in smoke by the drawing-room fire. But I have more confidence in her sterling good sense, and if I cannot be loved for what I am, I do not wish to be loved for what I am not.

---

===Chinese===

旧的分类体系：拉菲内斯克的“五进制”理论，斯温森的“七进制”理论，爱德华·纽曼那本名为《*Sphinx Vespiformis*》的书——在动物界处处追寻五，还有托马斯·布朗爵士的“五点梅花”（Quincunx）——在整个自然界追逐五——用柯尔律治的话说：“天上有五点梅花，地下有五点梅花，人心里有五点梅花，在视神经里，在树根里，在叶子里，在一切里！”

旧时的歧路：

点金石（巴尔塔扎·克拉斯）。26

万灵药（贝克莱主教的焦油水）。神秘的数字（同上）。

我的父亲是托马斯·布朗爵士，我的母亲是玛丽·巴什基尔采夫。瞧瞧我是个多么古怪的杂交产物！

---

我把这几页纸扔到那些怯懦、鬼祟、体面的人的脸上，说：“喏！这就是我！你们爱喜欢不喜欢，反正这是真的。我倒要请你们照样来一遍，把你们自我意识的探照灯，射进你们生活最幽暗的每个角落，请众人来参观检查。坦白些，诚实些，拆掉你们小隔间的隔板，从你们的地洞里爬出来吧，小虫子。”既然我们全是这样的虫子，那至少也该做诚实的虫子。

---

E——把我从伦敦生活的可怕苦楚中拽了出来，我的感激无以言表。倘若我是廉价女士小说里的男主角，就该把我的日记付之一炬以表情意，你们也就看到一幅好看的图景：一个面色苍白的年轻人，在客厅壁炉旁眼看着自己的岁月化为青烟。但我更信得过她纯金般的健全判断，倘若我不能因我本来的样子被爱，我也不愿因我并非的样子被爱。

---

===Original===

Since the fateful Nov. 27th, my life has become entirely posthumous. I live now in the grave and am busy furnishing it with posthumous joys. I accept my fate with great content, my onetime restless ambition lies asleep now, my onetime, furious self-assertiveness is anaesthetised by this great War; the War and the discovery about my health together have plucked out of me that canker of self-obsession. I sit at home here in this country cottage in perfect isolation﻿—flattened out by a steam hammer (though it took Armageddon to do it!), yet as cheerful and busy as a Dormouse laying up store for the winter. For I am almost resigned to the issue in the knowledge that some day, someone will know, perhaps somebody will understand and﻿—immortal powers!﻿—even sympathise, “the quick heart quickening from the heart that’s still.”

July 19

Omniscience

An omniscient Caledonian asked me today:

“Where are the Celebes? Are they E. or N.E. of the Sandwich Group?”

I marked him down at once as my legitimate prey. Sitting back in my chair, I replied slowly in my most offensive manner:

“The Island of Celebes is of enormous size and curious shape situated in the Malay Archipelago.”

The Caledonian made no sign. Instead of grinning at his error and confessing to a “floater,” he endeavoured to carry on by remarking, “That of course would be N. of Papua,” just for all the world as if his error was a minor one of latitude and longitude.

Ignoring his comment, I continued:

“From the Zoogeographical point of view, Celebes is unequalled in importance, having the strangest fauna almost of any island on the face of the globe. Then there’s ‘Wallace’s Line,’ ” I said, being purposely obscure.

The Caledonian said nought but looked hurt. It was so obvious that he didn’t know, and it was so obvious that I knew that he didn’t know, that after my farcical truculence I expected the tension to dissolve in laughter. Yet it is hard for a Caledonian to say “God be merciful to me, ignorant devil that I am.” So I pursued him with more information about “Wallace’s Line,” with an insouciant air, as much as to say, “Wallace’s Line of course you heard discussed before you were breached.”

“Some do say, you know, that the Line is ‘all my eye and Betty Martin,’ e.g., R﻿—﻿—.”

===Chinese===

自那命中注定的11月27日起，我的生命已完全变成身后之事。我如今住在坟墓里，忙着用身后的欢乐装点它。我心满意足地接受自己的命运，昔日那颗躁动的野心如今沉睡，昔日那凶猛的自我张扬也被这场大战麻醉了；战争与关于我健康的发现一起，把我心中自我沉迷的那颗毒瘤连根拔去。我坐在这乡间小屋的家中，与世隔绝——被一柄汽锤砸扁了（虽然要动用汽锤，还得靠末日大战！），却仍像只储备冬粮的睡鼠一样快活而忙碌。因为我对结局几乎已经认命，只知道终有一天，有人会知道，或许有人会理解，而且——不朽的神明啊！——甚至会生怜悯之心，“活跳的心，因那已静止的心而愈发悸动。”

7月19日

全知

一位无所不知的苏格兰人今天问我：

“西里伯斯岛在哪儿？在桑威奇群岛以东还是东北？”

我立刻把他标定为我的合法猎物。我靠回椅子里，用我最无礼的腔调慢条斯理地答道：

“西里伯斯岛幅员极广，形状古怪，位于马来群岛。”

那苏格兰人毫无表示。他不对自己的错误咧嘴一笑、认一句“考砸了”，反而设法接着往下说：“那当然是在巴布亚以北了，”那副样子，就仿佛他错的不过是纬度经度上的小节。

我不理他的评语，继续说道：

“从动物地理学的观点看，西里伯斯的重要性无与伦比，拥有的动物区系之奇特，几乎冠于全球诸岛。此外还有‘华莱士线’，”我说，故意装得高深莫测。

那苏格兰人不吭一声，只是面露委屈。他不知道，这是明摆着的；我心里清楚他不知道，这也明摆着的；闹剧般的凶横过后，我本以为这阵紧张会在哄笑中冰释。然而，要一个苏格兰人说一句“上帝怜悯我这条无知的可怜虫”，谈何容易。于是我继续拿“华莱士线”的种种知识追击他，一副满不在乎的神气，仿佛在说：“华莱士线嘛，你还在襁褓里的时候，当然就听人讨论过了。”

“要知道，有些人确实说，这条线是‘胡说八道’，比如R——。”

===Original===

This gave him his first opportunity of finding his feet in this perilously deep water. So he said promptly, eager to seem knowledgeable with an intelligent rejoinder:

“As! yes, R﻿—﻿— is an authority on Fishes.”

I assented. “At the last meeting of the British Ass. he tore the idea to shreds.”

The drowning Caledonian seized at any straw:

“Fishes, however, are not of paramount importance in cases of geographical distribution, are they?”

I knew he was thinking of *marine* fishes, but I did not illumine him, and merely said:

“Oh! yes, of very great importance,” at which he looked still more hurt, decamped in silence and left me conqueror of the field but without the spoils of victory: it was impossible to bring him to say “I do not know”﻿—four monosyllables was all I wanted from the man who for months past has been lecturing me on all things from Music and the Drama to Philosophy, Painting and﻿—Insects.

July 20

The cradle came a few days ago but I had not seen it until this morning when I unlocked the cupboard door, looked in and shuddered.

“That’s the skeleton in our cupboard,” I said on coming down to breakfast. She laughed, but I really meant it.

E﻿—﻿— keeps a blue bowl replenished with flaming Poppies in our room. The cottage is plagued with Earwigs which fly in at night and get among the clothes and bedlinen. This morning, dressing, she held up her chemise to the light saying: “I always do this﻿—you can see their little heathen bodies then against the light.﻿ ﻿…” Isn’t she charming?

July 30

The other day R﻿—﻿— and I were sitting on a stile on the uplands in perfect summer weather and talking of happy days before the War﻿—he was in khaki and I was resting my “gammy” leg.﻿ ﻿… As we talked, we let our eyes roam, resting luxuriously wherever we pleased and occasionally interrupting the conversation with “Look at that cow scratching herself against the Oak,” or “Do you see the oats waving?” In the distance we saw a man and a boy walking up towards us along the path through the corn, but the eye having momentarily scrutinised them wandered away and the conversation never paused. When next I looked, they were much nearer﻿—crossing the furrows in the potato field in fact, and we both stopped talking to watch﻿—idly. The boy seemed to be about 10 years old, and it amused us to see his great difficulty in stepping across the furrows.

===Chinese===

这给了他头一个在这片险恶深水里踩到底的机会。于是他立刻接话，急欲显得有见识，回了一句聪明话：

“啊！是的，R——是鱼类学权威。”

我表示同意。“在上届英国科学促进会（British Ass.）的会议上，他把这个观点驳得体无完肤。”

溺水将没的苏格兰人赶忙乱抓稻草：

“不过，就地理分布而言，鱼类算不上头等重要吧？”

我知道他心里想的是*海*鱼，但我不点破，只说：

“哦！不，至关重要，”听了这话，他愈发委屈，一言不发地撤退了，留给我一座空荡荡的战场，却没有战利品：我没法让他说出“我不知道”——我一心只想从这个人嘴里抠出这四个字，几个月来，他一直在给我上课，从音乐、戏剧一直讲到哲学、绘画，还有——昆虫。

7月20日

摇篮几天前就送来了，可我一直没去看，直到今天早晨我打开橱柜门朝里一望，不禁一颤。

“这就是我们家柜子里的骷髅，”下楼吃早饭时我说。她笑了，可我是当真的。

E——在我们房里养着一只蓝碗，里面不断添满红艳艳的罂粟花。小屋里蠼螋为患，夜里飞进来，钻到衣服和床单里。今天早晨穿衣时，她把衬裙举起来对着光说：“我回回都这样——迎着光就能看见它们那些异教徒小身子。……”她可不可爱？

7月30日

前几天，R——和我在晴美无伦的夏日里，坐在高地上一道跨越栅栏的阶梯上，聊着战前的快乐时光——他穿着卡其军装，我搁着我那条“瘸”腿。……我们聊着，任眼睛四处漫游，看到哪儿舒坦就在哪儿流连，时不时插上一句“瞧那头在橡树上蹭痒的母牛”，或者“看见燕麦在风里起伏吗？”远处我们望见一个男人和一个男孩顺着麦田间的小路朝我们走来，但目光略一打量便游走了，谈话并未中断。待我再望时，他们已近了许多——其实正在穿过马铃薯田的垄沟，我们便都住了口看着——心不在焉地看。那男孩看上去十岁上下，见他迈过垄沟那般吃力，我们只觉得好笑。

===Original===

“Poor little chap,” R﻿—﻿— said, and we laughed.

Then the boy stumbled badly and all at once the man lifted his walking-stick and beat him, saying ill-naturedly, “Step between the furrows,” and again, “Step between the furrows.” Our enchanting little picture was transfigured in an instant. The “charming little boy” was a natural idiot﻿—a gross, hefty creature perhaps 30 years of age, very short and very thick, dressed in a little sailor suit. I said, “Heavens,” and R﻿—﻿— looked positively scared. We stood aside for them to get over the stile, the “boy” still suffering from his over exertion, breathing stertorously like a horse pulling uphill and still evidently fearful of the big stick behind. He scrambled over the stile as best he could, rolling a wild eye at us as he did so﻿—a large, bulgy eye with the lower lid swollen and sore, like the eye of a terrified ox on the way to the slaughter house. So much then for our little picture of charming childhood! The man followed close at his heels and looked at me with stern defiant eyes. “Yes, that is my son,” his eyes declaimed, “and I’ll thank you to avert your gaze or by the Lord I’ll beat you too.”

A Yellow Cat

Last week, I saw a yellow cat perched up quite high on a window ledge at the S﻿—﻿— Underground Station in celestial detachment from the crowd of serious, black-coated gentlemen hustling along to and from the trains. He had his back turned to us, but as I swept past in the stream, I was forced to look back a moment, and caught the outline of his whiskers﻿—it made me smile intensely to myself and secretly I gave the palm to the cat for wisdom.

July 31

This War is so great and terrible that hyperbole is impossible.
